# WebUI ledger: every recorded WebUI problem and its state

**The one state source for the WebUI's known problems** (rev 2 completion plan, 2026-11-16, T00). BACKLOG items and the round README point here by ID; a plan names the IDs it takes. Base: `ba6e85e`. Built from the audit of 2026-11-16, which checked every recorded WebUI problem against `ba6e85e` in the running app (a CPU demo workspace in mode M1) or in the code, then had a second reviewer look for missing items and overturn verdicts; the audit's evidence files were not kept in the repository, its findings are restated here.

Sources read: `docs/working/WEBUI_UX_REVIEW_2026-10-25.md` (UX) and its `review.json` (RJ), `docs/working/BACKLOG.md` (BL), the round README `docs/working/ops-wiring-and-ui-ux/README.md` (OPS), the skill `.claude/skills/webui-design/SKILL.md` (SK), the M5 WebUI acceptance (M5W), `docs/working/PLAN_TEST_RESULTS.md` (PTR), `docs/working/scorer-retraining/README.md` (SR), and the owner's fix list rev 1 (2026-11-16).

## States

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| to do | open, not started in this round |
| in progress | being changed in this round |
| partly done | some of it is done; `Done when` says what is left |
| code-verified | done, with the commit and the tests or browser checks that show it |
| code-verified at ba6e85e (audit) | already done before this round; the audit saw it |
| awaiting hardware | needs a CUDA device or the GB10 to check |
| awaiting owner acceptance | done in code; the owner's own check of the screen is pending |
| not applicable | its subject is retired or gone; the reason names the source |
| outside this round's scope (owner to decide) | a new product surface, not a defect of an existing screen; kept here, not dropped |

A row is closed only with its scope, its tests and the screen it was seen on; a fix on one page is never read as a site-wide fix.

## The counts, reconciled

- **218 rows**: 183 recorded problems (the audit's inventory of 163 and 20 the second reviewer found missing from it) and 35 new findings of the 2026-11-16 audit.
- **The 2026-10-25 review's 83 findings are 77 rows** (`R-` IDs): finding 1 is split into 1a (the label), 1b (the repeat), 1c (the evidence marks) and 1d (the phenotype count); nine findings are merged into another (37 into 22; 40 and 75 into 18; 43 into 10; 46 into 20; 50 into 19; 51 into 8; 56 into 17; 76 into 16). At `ba6e85e`: 16 fixed, 34 partly, 25 not done (14 of them kept open by no record at all), 2 not applicable. The not-applicable two (R-44 and R-65, retired features) are why 16 + 34 + 25 reported to the owner made 75, not 77.
- **The owner's own reports and rechecks are 33 rows**: 10 fixed, 10 partly, 7 not done, 5 new in the fix list rev 1, 1 awaiting hardware. Several are also review rows (R-22+R-37, R-38, R-42, R-47, R-1b), so these counts overlap the review's and are not added to them.
- **The 35 new findings** (`NEW-` IDs): 18 restate a recorded row (`Duplicate of` names it), 17 are new problems. They overlap the counts above and are not added to them.
- **Overstated records**: eight rows a record called done that were only partly done (three verdicts overturned by the second reviewer: R-18+R-40+R-75+B68+B72, R-66+B51f, R-72+B72; and R-25+K6, RC-B, B72-N7, R-16+R-76+B72, R-53).
- **By state at the base**: 97 to do, 49 partly done, 59 already done, 1 awaiting hardware, 5 not applicable, 7 outside this round's scope.

#### NEW-shell-nav-theme-1 · Chosen in-page radio fill is exactly the level-1 switch's fill

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-11-16 audit · page: shell
- **Duplicate of**: R-20+R-46+B72 (closed with it)
- **At ba6e85e**: Close fix 3d85531 made the chosen in-page radio's fill exactly the level-1 switch's fill: --checkbox-label-background-fill-selected is var(--shep-accent) (src/webui/style.py:240), the same as the selected level-1 tab (style.py:113-114, both rgb(85,88,227) in theme-1850x960.json and shell1-1850x960.json). The likeness BACKLOG 72 'left open' became an exact match, and the close record (OPS:364) mentions only the 5.4:1 contrast.
- **Done when**: The selected level-1 tab and a selected in-page radio have different computed styles (fill or shape), checked in a browser at 1850x960.
- **Sources**: found by the 2026-11-16 audit at ba6e85e
- **Re-checked (T04 measure, a569948)**: the navigation test still pins the two fills and passes; the shared check does not compare the levels' looks, it finds a focused radio's cue (its token border) and a focused level-1 tab's (its 2 px token ring) at 3:1 on every page at every size.
- **Closed at**: d3203dd, closed with R-20+R-46+B72. A chosen in-page radio is a tinted option (accent-50 fill, accent border and dot, body-colour text), no longer the level-1 accent fill with white text. Seen in Chromium at 1850x960 on Training: Research tab rgb(85, 88, 227) with white text; Device "auto" rgb(238, 240, 255) with rgb(29, 34, 48) text and an rgb(85, 88, 227) border. Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_the_research_navigation_and_shared_tokens_as_drawn (the fills differ, computed) and tests/unit/test_webui_button_contrast.py::test_a_chosen_radios_label_reads_on_its_fill_and_is_not_the_level_1_look. #### R-15 · Two navigation rows plus footer take 131px of a 964px window

- **State**: partly done · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity medium · page: shell
- **Problem**: Two navigation rows plus the footer took 131 px of 964, out of the chart grid (evidence cites the owner's 'charts squeezed' complaint).
- **At ba6e85e**: At 1850x960 the header row is 0-55 px, the research tab row 55-100 px and the runtime footer 928-960 px: 132 of 960 px (the review measured 131 of 964). The research tabs are still a second row. The footer is still its own 32 px row, now leading with the consequence. Training's four plots are 230/217 px. At 960 px wide the header wraps to 99 px, with a 45 px tab row under it.
- **Done when**: At 1850x960 the research tabs sit in the header row (and/or the footer is folded into it), so navigation and footer together take clearly less than 132 px and Training's chart grid gains that height.
- **Sources**: UX:217 (appendix 15); RJ audits[0].findings[16]; UX:119-120 (section 4: research tabs inside the header row when there is room); RJ research[1].principles[1] (G2)
- **Re-checked (T04 measure, a569948)**: the shared check (a569948: the service on the demo workspace, a base and its disease child trained and the child served, an operator; every page at 1850x960, 1280x900, 1920x700, 1440x1600, 960x1080, 390x844 and 1536x760): header and tabs cause no sideways page scroll at any size and the captions stay off the tabs' line; header and tabs still 102 px at 1850x960 (the navigation test). The height, the owner's choice above, is unchanged.
- **Owner's decision (verified T06-D)**: the height is the owner's choice; no code waits on it. Measured on this branch at 1850x960 on Training: header 0-47 px, research tabs 47-102 px (the group captions' 18 px tier over 32 px tabs), footer 928-960 px: 134 px. The choices and what each changes: (a) keep D01's two rows and the footer row: nothing changes and the row closes as decided (134 px against the review's 131); (b) drop the caption tier: about 22 px back to every research page, the Prepare / Evaluate captions gone or put back on the tabs' line, which the owner rejected (skill §3); (c) fold the footer into the header: 32 px back, but its line (822 px) does not fit the header's free span (about 713 px), so its words are cut or row 1 wraps, or its consequence moves behind a bubble (skill §7: never for a must-know fact); (d) the research tabs in the header row (the Done when's first way): the 55 px row back, a change of D01's navigation whose fit at narrower windows is not measured here.
- **Closed at**: d3203dd (D01), with the footer decision measured and recorded; the row's Done when is not met. Measured at 1850x960 on Training: header 0-47 px and research tabs 47-102 px, so header and tabs take 102 px (D01's about 100; 100 before, when the group labels sat on the tab line); the footer stays its own 32 px row (928-960), so the three take 134 px against 132 before, and the four plots stay 231/231/217/217 px. **Footer decision: kept as its own row.** Its line (the consequence, 749 px, then Versions ›, 61 px: 822 px with the gap) does not fit the header's free span between the stage switch and the chips (538 to 1299 px less the gaps: about 713 px), so folding it in would cut words to an ellipsis or wrap row 1, losing what it says; a chip with an ⓘ would hide a must-know consequence behind a bubble (skill §7). Left: the owner's choice between D01's caption tier (2 px more than before) and the row's "clearly less than 132 px", which needs the footer folded or the caption tier dropped. Screens: scratchpad/t04/nav-tokens/shots/{before,after}/*_training_1850x960.png.

#### R-20+R-46+B72 · Level-1 switch and in-page radios share one filled accent style

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity medium · page: shell
- **Problem**: The Diagnosis/Research switch looked like a selected form radio, in-page radios copied its filled style, and Settings had a fourth navigation look.
- **At ba6e85e**: The level-1 switch's selected 'Research (pre-clinical)' is a #5558e3 fill with white 700 text. Training's chosen Device 'auto' and Fine-tune's chosen Graph backbone 'Trained' have exactly the same #5558e3 fill with white text, and neither uses the .shep-seg third style. Close fix 3d85531 caused the exact match: it set --checkbox-label-background-fill-selected to var(--shep-accent) to fix contrast. When Settings is selected it has a fourth look (an accent-50 box with an inset underline), and the level-1 switch then shows neither stage as selected. The level-1 tablist is still named 'Stage'. R-46's label half is fixed (b33cb14).
- **Done when**: The selected level-1 switch option looks different from a selected in-page radio (Training Device, Fine-tune Graph backbone use .shep-seg or another style), and Settings' selected look fits the navigation levels; a computed-style test shows the fills differ.
- **Sources**: UX:222 (appendix 20); RJ audits[0].findings[7]; UX:248 (appendix 46); RJ audits[1].findings[13]; UX:54 (section 2 layer 4); BL:1030 (item 72 Shell); SK:48-50
- **Re-checked (T04 measure, a569948)**: the navigation test passes; the shared check finds every level's focus cue at 3:1 on every page at every size (the tabs' 2 px token ring, a radio's token border, and since 71b8bac Gradio's buttons, which drew the browser's own ring: R-52+B52L).
- **Closed at**: d3203dd. Level 1's selected item, the stage switch's segment and Settings alike, is the one accent-filled look (white text, 600); level 2 is an underline, level 3 (.shep-seg) a raised white segment, and a form's chosen radio (Training's Device, Fine-tune's Graph backbone) a tinted option with its accent dot and border and body-colour text, so no two levels share a treatment. Settings' selected look is level 1's (it was a fourth look, a tint with an inset underline). Seen in Chromium at 1850x960: Research and selected Settings rgb(85, 88, 227) with white text; Device "auto" rgb(238, 240, 255) with dark text. Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_the_research_navigation_and_shared_tokens_as_drawn; tests/unit/test_webui_button_contrast.py::test_a_chosen_radios_label_reads_on_its_fill_and_is_not_the_level_1_look (both fail on c1e57c6). The level-1 tablist is still named "Stage" for a screen reader (unchanged). Screens: scratchpad/t04/nav-tokens/shots/{before,after}/*_training_1850x960.png, *_settings_1850x960.png.

#### R-22+R-37 · PREPARE/EVALUATE group labels sit on the tab row and read as tabs

- **State**: code-verified · found by the owner · severity medium · page: shell
- **Problem**: The PREPARE and EVALUATE group labels are ::before content on the first tab of each group, on the tabs' 44 px row at about the same size and weight, so they read as two more (clickable) tabs.
- **At ba6e85e**: Unchanged. 'Prepare' and 'Evaluate' are ::before content of the first tab in each group. They are absolutely positioned left of that tab, inside the same 44 px row and centred on the tabs' line, at 14 px/600 uppercase with .07em spacing in #5b6270 (6.13:1). The tabs beside them are 15 px/500 #434a58, so the labels are heavier than the unselected tabs. The gap from PREPARE to Datasets is about 18 px, against 24 px between tabs, and the offsets are still hard-coded margins (84 px and 116 px). The accessibility half is met: the labels sit outside the tabs' hit area (elementsFromPoint returns the tablist; the cursor is auto), have pointer-events none, are not focusable, and the tablist's aria-label names the groups. The visual half the owner reported, that they read as tabs, is not. At 480 px the EVALUATE group wraps onto its own line, with its divider at the line's start. The CSS predates the shallow history's root 9a96a72 (2026-10-30), no commit in 30e8ae1..ba6e85e touched it, and no test pins its look (tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py:74 checks only the aria-label). The shell docstring (shell.py:17-23) still describes it as is.
- **Done when**: PREPARE and EVALUATE no longer sit on the tabs' line: they are placed apart (e.g. as overlines or with a clear gap and divider) at 14 px, distinguished by position, spacing and weight, without hard-coded pixel margins; a browser test checks their vertical offset from the tabs.
- **Sources**: UX:224 (appendix 22); RJ audits[0].findings[2]; UX:239 (appendix 37); RJ audits[1].findings[19]; UX:53-54 (section 2 layer 4); UX:119 (section 4 site-wide); UX:152 (section 5 decision 2); RJ audits[0].overall (point 4); RJ research[1].principles[0] (G1); RJ research[4].principles[7] (S8); RJ audits[2].findings[15] (R-76 principle); SK:51-52; SK:187-188
- **Evidence (T04 measure, a569948)**: the shared check (a569948: the service on the demo workspace, a base and its disease child trained and the child served, an operator; every page at 1850x960, 1280x900, 1920x700, 1440x1600, 960x1080, 390x844 and 1536x760), on every research page: each caption ends above its tabs' line, overlaps no tab, is hit-tested as the tablist (not a tab), takes no pointer and is 14 px; wrapped at 390x844 too. The same check fails on c1e57c6 for both captions at all 30 research-page stops (on the tabs' line: bottom 93 against the line's top 55 at 1850x960). Test: tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_every_page_at_every_size_passes_the_ui_measurement_check. Screens: scratchpad/t04/measure/shots/after/matrix/<page>_<w>x<h>.png.
- **Closed at**: d3203dd (D01 option (e); the mockups are not owner-approved, so the owner's look is still to come). "Prepare" and "Evaluate" are sentence-case captions on a tier of their own above the first tab of each group, aligned with its text (12 px in), 14 px / 600 in the muted token (6.13:1), 2 px above the 32 px tabs; the groups are a group gap (32 px token) apart with a 1 px divider; no fitted margins (84/116 px gone). Measured in Chromium at 1850x960: captions 18 px tall ending at the tabs' top (y 49-67 against tabs at 69), elementsFromPoint at a caption's centre finds the tablist, not a tab; pointer-events none, not focusable. Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_the_research_navigation_and_shared_tokens_as_drawn (vertical offset, alignment, size, weight, case, hit test) and tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_the_group_captions_are_sentence_case_overlines_without_fitted_margins; both fail on c1e57c6. The shell docstring says it as it is now. Screens: scratchpad/t04/nav-tokens/shots/{before,after}/*_training_1850x960.png, *_nav_hover_1850x960.png, *_nav_focus_1850x960.png.

### UI-02 Orientation and scrolling

#### FL-UI-02 · Layout picks side-by-side vs stacked from fixed 1400x760 sizes, not the window's shape

- **State**: partly done · found by the owner · severity unrated (fix list) · page: shell
- **Problem**: The panels sit side by side only at 1400 px wide or more and 760 px tall or more; otherwise they stack (style.py:443-478). The fix list's counterexamples follow from this: 1280 x 900 and 1920 x 700 stack, while 1440 x 1600 (portrait) is side by side. A short-height window is forced into the stacked layout although it has the width.
- **At ba6e85e**: Recorded only in the fix list rev 1 (2026-11-16), confirmed there from the code. Earlier records state the rule (by the usable size), but none records the fixed threshold as a problem. Not fixed.
- **Done when**: At 1920x700 and 1280x900-wide-landscape windows the panels sit side by side and at 1440x1600 portrait they stack; short height is handled by region scrolling with no control covered, checked by a browser test over these sizes.
- **Sources**: the owner's fix list rev 1 (2026-11-16); src/webui/style.py:443-478; docs/working/BACKLOG.md:985 (item 27: 'by the browser's usable size rather than the monitor's'); docs/working/PLAN_TEST_RESULTS.md:2390-2394
- **Evidence (T04 measure, a569948)**: the layout test now reads its facts from the shared walk (the service itself on the demo workspace, a disease child served; every page through 760x1000, 1850x960, 1280x900, 1920x700, 1440x1600, 1000x1000, 1536x760, 1080x1920, 960x1080, 390x844 and back to 1850x960, then Training, Datasets and Fine-tune opened at 1280x900): the same decisions, 960x1080 and 390x844 stacked, Datasets stacked at 1280x900 whether resized to or opened at, each page's first and last 1850x960 alike. Nothing over a focused control is now the shared check's, every control on every page at 1850x960, 1280x900, 1920x700, 1440x1600, 960x1080, 390x844 and 1536x760, not two short sizes: it found the Top-K and Explanations bubbles over their own ⓘ (R-24) and the run bar's fingerprint ⓘ under its bubble when stacked (R-38+owner), both fixed. Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_one_layout_decision_at_every_size_and_back, tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_every_page_at_every_size_passes_the_ui_measurement_check. Screens: scratchpad/t04/measure/shots/after/matrix/<page>_<w>x<h>.png.
- **Progress (T04 layout-rows, 497f583)**: The 15 orientation media queries and 2 matchMedia calls at 9b7d9b6 (style.py 2, finetune 1, diagnosis 3, models 2 + SCROLL_JS, datasets 1, training 3 + _BANNER_JS, test 2, charts 1) are one decision in the shell's layout pass: the window's inner size (no wider than tall: stack), then whether the shown page's panels fit by their own --shep-panel-min (read from the panels as drawn, with their gaps); written as data-shep-layout on the root element, which every style sheet nests its rules in (style.SIDE/STACK/SIDE_SHORT); data-shep-short under 900 px is density only. Content breakpoints kept: Diagnosis' input width (1600/1800) and one-column fields (760), Datasets' short labels (1799), Settings' name column (899), reduced motion; Training's 1199 run-bar rule went with its one-row bar. Measured on the CPU demo (4 test reports): 1280x900 side by side on every page but Datasets (its own minimum, about 1400 px: the six-column table 860 px and the four-column digest grid 495 px; whether a more compact table should bring it side by side at 1280 is the owner's call), 1920x700 side by side (short), 1440x1600, 1000x1000, 1080x1920, 960x1080 and 760x1000 stacked, 1850x960/1920x1080/1536x760 side by side; no page-level horizontal scroll at any size; nothing sticky over a focused control (the only cover the matrix found is the ⓘ help bubble of the control focused just before, the same as at 9b7d9b6). Also from the brief: the header chips end in a titled ellipsis inside the window (at 360 px the Operator controls chip's left edge was -41, now 16; ::test_no_header_element_covers_a_tab_at_any_width at 360 and 420); stacked, a table wider than its panel scrolls sideways in its own box (Datasets at 760x1000: 692/822, was cut by 94 px). Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_one_layout_decision_at_every_size_and_back (an operator; 760x1000 → 1850x960 → 1280x900 → 1920x700 → 1440x1600 → 1000x1000 → 1536x760 → 1080x1920 → 1850x960, every page), ::test_one_layout_decision_keys_every_style_sheet_and_no_window_size_gate_is_left; both fail on 9b7d9b6 (attribute absent, 1280x900 stacked). - **Progress (T04 review fixes, 796f9bc and 3d5a4cd)**: the review found the row closed for less than its Done when: Datasets stacks at 1280x900. The brief's first step was tried on its table, measured in Chromium on the demo at 1280x900: without the Sources and In use floors (7 and 11 em) the table's own minimum is still 734 px in the 701 px its panel has there (Name 173, Built 148, Dataset fingerprint 157, Sources 72, samples 75, In use 110 px; the two In use marks then wrap to three lines each), so side by side would need about 1281 px of room against 1248 (with the floors 860 + 495 + 16 = 1371); none of the widths left is a fixed one (Name is its nowrap origin and record line, Built and the fingerprint their nowrap values), so nothing was changed. #### B39-UI2 · Model list reads every checkpoint though it shows only four runs

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · page: models
- **Problem**: Reads more than it shows (M5-3 UI-2).
- **At ba6e85e**: model_list.list_models calls server_lists.list_checkpoints(chosen) for the whole workspace (model_list.py:555). That reads every run's checkpoints (server_lists.py:696, read_run_checkpoints over all runs, cached per file after the first read). The clinical preset ('Change model…') shows only the latest 4 runs (model_list.py:248, latest_runs=4; :761) from that full read. list_run_checkpoints is not used for a read-free listing. The demo has 2 runs, so the cost cannot show (inferred from code).
- **Done when**: A unit test shows the first listing and Change model read only the runs shown, and more only after 'Show older runs'.
- **Sources**: BL:997 (item 39)
- **Closed at**: 572c34f, 11bbe99. The drawer's first listing reads only the latest runs it shows, and the run of the model in use when that is older, from a run list that opens no checkpoint (`server_lists.listed_runs`); "Show older runs (up to N more)" reads the rest. The Models page still reads every run, because it shows every run and By family needs every file's recorded parent. Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_model_list.py::test_the_drawer_opens_only_the_runs_it_shows_and_the_rest_on_show_older_runs (counts the checkpoint files opened: six at first, the older runs' only after Show older runs; on aad658c it fails because every file, base.pt and model-07.pt included, is opened), tests/unit/test_checkpoint_paths.py::TestLinksUnderRuns::test_only_the_runs_asked_for_are_read. Seen in Chromium on a multi-family CPU demo (2 families, 10 runs, 20 files) at 1850x960 and 760x1000: the drawer reads "Latest 4 runs, 8 saved files" over "Show older runs (up to 4 more)" (before: "newest first · latest 4 runs · 8 checkpoints (of 14 in 7 runs)", every file read). Screens: scratchpad/t05/list-core/shots/before_drawer_1850x960.png, scratchpad/t05/list-core/shots/after_drawer_1850x960.png (and _760x1000). **Review fixes (T05 review, 2026-11-17)**: 59eef54 — "Show older runs" was kept for the session, so a workspace picked after it opened every checkpoint; it is now asked of one workspace (the shared refresh keeps it only while the workspace is the one it was asked of, as it keeps the folds; reading the same workspace again keeps it). 59ea42c — while runs are not read, the search and the Task filter say they looked in the latest runs, and "Show older runs" stays under an empty result (below, FL-UI-04). Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_model_list.py::test_the_drawer_opens_only_the_runs_it_shows_and_the_rest_on_show_older_runs (another workspace after Show older runs opens its six latest files only, the state back to False; on 9b160ab all nine files are opened). Seen in Chromium on the multi-family demo (gene-frozen served) and a copy of it: after Show older runs on demo, picking the copy read "7 runs, 14 saved files" with no Show older runs at 9b160ab, "Latest 4 runs, 8 saved files" with "Show older runs (up to 4 more)" now, at 1850x960 and 760x1000. Screens: scratchpad/t05/fixer/shots/before_drawer_other_workspace_1850x960.png, scratchpad/t05/fixer/shots/after_drawer_other_workspace_1850x960.png (and _760x1000); scratchpad/t05/fixer/logs/cases_before.json, scratchpad/t05/fixer/logs/cases_after.json. Kept as decided: closing and opening the drawer on the same workspace keeps "Show older runs" with the folds and the selection (a selection in an older run stays listed).

The visible reason reads 'metadata not readable · cannot be loaded' even for a directory that was never read.

#### FL-UI-04 · Models list has no folding or search, and files of one run read alike

- **State**: code-verified · found by the owner · severity unrated (fix list) · page: models
- **Problem**: Every saved file is its own tall row, and families and runs cannot be folded. No search by name or fingerprint exists. Two files of one run read identically: on the shared demo both demo-disease rows read 'Disease demo-disease · GATv2 2026-11-16 09:13 1 epoch completed', and only the fingerprint and validation cells differ. Under By family all four files are listed flat under one family title (c3-models-byfamily.json). The backend already has exact parent links (server_lists family_rows), as the fix list notes.
- **At ba6e85e**: Fix list rev 1. It overlaps RC-B, whose fold was asked of the shared model list and not built (RC-B verdict partly), and RC-D and BACKLOG 31 (not started). No search exists.
- **Done when**: Families and runs show as foldable summary rows (kind, task, file count); each file row shows save kind and epoch so two files of one run read differently; a name/fingerprint search filters while keeping the family title; the selection survives fold, filter and refresh.
- **Sources**: the owner's fix list rev 1 (2026-11-16); docs/working/WEBUI_UX_REVIEW_2026-10-25.md:135-137 (recheck (B): 'fold each run's checkpoints ... in the shared model list'); docs/working/WEBUI_UX_REVIEW_2026-10-25.md:139-140 (recheck (D)); docs/working/BACKLOG.md:989 (item 31: search, archiving)
- **Closed at**: 11bbe99 (D04). The research list is a run tree drawn by one renderer for both orders (`model_list.tree_view`, `_tree_html`): under By family each family is a summary row ("Family from demo-base · 8 runs, 16 saved files") and a row group of its own; each run a summary row (its name and start, Task, Operation with the three freeze words "Fine-tune, full / frozen / partly frozen" from the recorded weight transfer, epochs completed, number of saved files; no scores), placed under the run of its exact parent file (the server's SHA-256 match); its files folded under it, each by what it is ("Latest save", "Best pooled MRR" from the recorded selection metric and direction) with its own fingerprint, each still selectable. The selected and the served model's family and run open and are scrolled to; folds are kept for the session and never change the selected key. A search box (name, fingerprint prefix or full value, case ignored) keeps the matches and the runs they come from (said, quieter), opens what it found, keeps the Task filter and the families, and Clear restores; a selection a filter or search hides is said ("Selected model not shown by this filter (Task: Gene): …; its details are kept"). A refresh keeps a still-valid selection, its details and the folds. Newest first draws the same run rows without families. Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_model_list.py::test_the_tree_folds_runs_and_unfolds_the_selected_and_the_served, ::test_a_search_finds_names_and_fingerprints_and_keeps_where_they_come_from, ::test_a_search_unfolds_what_it_found, ::test_the_refresh_keeps_the_selection_and_the_folds_and_a_lone_row_is_chosen, ::test_a_fold_toggle_is_kept_and_draws_nothing_again, tests/unit/test_webui_models_page.py::test_a_refresh_keeps_the_selection_and_its_detail (fails with the refresh's detail left blank, shown by reverting the fix), ::test_the_tree_folds_searches_filters_and_keeps_the_selection_in_a_browser (real events in Chromium; also the table fits its frame and splits no word at 1850x960 and 760x1000, which failed on the first tree: 48 px wider than its frame, "Dise/ase"). Seen in Chromium on a multi-family CPU demo (2 families, 10 runs, 20 files) at 1850x960 and 760x1000: "2 families, 10 runs, 20 saved files", 2 row groups, files folded, 24x24 toggles, no sideways scroll; choosing a file opens its run; "disease" gives "5 runs match “disease”, with 2 runs they come from"; the fingerprint prefix E6B7D8 opens a family folded by hand; Gene keeps the selection's details and says it is outside; leaving and coming back keeps the selection, its details and the folds; the served file ("In use (Diagnosis)", loaded on CPU) is open and in view. Screens: scratchpad/t05/list-core/shots/before_models_family_1850x960.png, scratchpad/t05/list-core/shots/after_models_family_default_1850x960.png, after_models_family_selected_, after_models_search_name_, after_models_search_fingerprint_, after_models_selected_outside_filter_, after_models_refreshed_, after_models_served_located_ (each _1850x960 and _760x1000). **Review fixes (T05 review, 2026-11-17)**: 59ea42c — the drawer's search and Task filter see the latest runs it read only (B39-UI2); a query matching only an older run read "No model finetuned for a task matches “5d5252” in this workspace." with no Show older runs (the demo-gene run exists). While runs are not read, the empty state says "… in the latest runs; Show older runs searches the rest." with Show older runs under it, the count "No run matches “5d5252” in the latest runs", "1 run matches “gene” in the latest runs", a filter "Disease: 2 of the latest 4 runs"; once read, the search finds it. 91b728a — the tree did not fit its frame at 1280x900 (Newest first 565 px in 531, By family 609; the Fingerprint cut) nor By family at 1366x620: Models' list minimum was the old table's 450 px; it is now the tree's measured width with its widest common rows (692 px), and the demo's tree is 656/656 at 1280x900. 393cfe5 — an unreadable file's row named the file twice, joined three facts with two " · " and gave its reason in a title only; it reads "broken.pt | Metadata not readable: the file cannot be read as a checkpoint; cannot be loaded" (the loader's reference left to the details). #### B31 · No archiving from the lists and no search across workspaces

- **State**: outside this round's scope (owner to decide) · found by an internal review · page: models
- **Problem**: BACKLOG 31 records three things beyond M5's Models page: a side-by-side comparison (built since as Results › Compare, research-comparison Stage 8b), archiving checkpoints from the lists while keeping them referenceable, and search across workspaces ('which checkpoints were trained on dataset fingerprint X'). Archiving and search do not exist at ba6e85e. OPS U3 excluded 'archive or delete tools' from the round.
- **At ba6e85e**: Recorded 2026-10-19. Compare is built; archive and search are not started. Only cited as a source of RC-D in the inventory.
- **Why this state**: Compare is built; what remains, archiving checkpoints and search across workspaces, are deferred new product surfaces in tasks_def.md. In-workspace name/fingerprint search is covered by FL-UI-04 (UI-04).
- **Owner's choice** (checked at T05, 2026-11-16; nothing built): whether either surface is wanted, and when. (a) **Archiving from the lists**: a checkpoint marked archived leaves the Models list and the Load for inference drawer by default, its file untouched, while every record, result, lineage parent and export that names it still finds it (an archive state the listing, the API and the shared list would carry); today nothing in the listing or the API marks a file archived. (b) **Search across workspaces** ("which checkpoints were trained on dataset fingerprint X"): the shared list's search (FL-UI-04, 11bbe99) finds names and fingerprints in the chosen workspace's listing only, on Models and in the drawer; a search over every workspace would read every workspace's runs (BACKLOG 39's cost) and needs a result view of its own.
- **Sources**: docs/working/BACKLOG.md:989 (item 31); docs/working/BACKLOG.md:183 (§0 'Later': '30, 31'); docs/working/ops-wiring-and-ui-ux/README.md:172 (U3)

#### T08-flow-4 · The model tree names a run's tied best saves and its top-k saves by file name

- **State**: code-verified · found by the T08 browser flow · severity low · page: models, diagnosis
- **Problem**: The tree (Models and Diagnosis' Load drawer) named a run's files tied at its best recorded figure, and two top-k saves of one run, by their file names, so no row of such a run said "Best", while Test's and Fine-tune's selects said "Best pooled MRR" for the same files.
- **At 71587aa**: b-ft-full-disease read "Latest save 3 · Top-k save 3 · model-01-0.8400.pt 2 · model-00-0.8400.pt 1": the two files at the recorded best (validation pooled MRR 0.84, both in best_k) carried no "Best pooled MRR". b-ft-frozen-disease named model-01-0.3333.pt and model-02-0.2700.pt by file though the listing marks both in_best_k (its model-00-0.4467.pt read "Best pooled MRR"). Test's and Fine-tune's selects for the same runs: "Best pooled MRR · 2 epochs completed". Evidence: scratchpad/t08/B/json/flow.json (b8-nodevice.rows_shown, p_loaddrawer.rows, s1_base and s4_measure ckpt_labels), scratchpad/t08/B/json/b8-checkpoint-selection-records.txt, scratchpad/t08/B/json/b9-final_api_v1_workspaces_demo_checkpoints.json, scratchpad/t08/B/shots/b6-00-results-initial-w.png.
- **Done when**: A unit test on a listing with a tie at best and two top-k saves of different epochs draws "Best …" on both tied rows and "Top-k save" on both, the epochs telling them apart, file names only where two rows would read exactly alike; it fails before. One browser look on Models.
- **Sources**: the T08 browser flow (scratchpad/t08/B/json/flow.json, scratchpad/t08/B/json/b8-checkpoint-selection-records.txt); SK:134-139 (§8: "best" read from the list's metadata, ties may share it)
- **Closed at**: a6980a9. Root: `model_list._file_names` fell back to file names on the "what" words alone, while the epochs that tell such rows apart are a column of their own; it also took best and the fallback over the files shown, so a search showing one top-k file of a run called it "Best pooled MRR". `checkpoint_choices` kept its own copy of the rule, keyed on both parts. Fix: one rule, `model_list._checkpoint_entries` (what a file is and how far it trained; best by the run's recorded selection, ties all; a file's name only where both parts read exactly alike), read by the selects and by the tree, over every file the listing lists in the run. Test: `test_webui_model_list.py::test_the_tree_names_tied_best_and_top_k_saves_as_its_selects_do` (both presets, a search too; fails on e0e4237, "model-01-0.8400.pt" where "Best pooled MRR" was expected). Seen in the real app on a copy of the T08 workspace (scratchpad/t08fix/U3/json/before-look.json, after-look.json): b-ft-full-disease now reads "Latest save 3 · Top-k save 3 · Best pooled MRR 2 · Best pooled MRR 1" and b-ft-frozen-disease "Latest save 3 · Top-k save 3 · Top-k save 2 · Best pooled MRR 1", on Models and in the Load drawer. Screens: scratchpad/t08fix/U3/shots/before-models-tree-w.png and after-models-tree-w.png (1850×960), the -n pair at 960×1080.

### UI-05 The ordering switch's events

1 rows: 1 code-verified.

#### FL-UI-05 · By family is selected while the table and count line read newest first

- **State**: code-verified · found by the owner · severity unrated (fix list) · page: models
- **Problem**: In the owner's screenshot, Order shows 'By family' selected, yet the count line reads 'newest first · 16 checkpoints in 8 runs'. The table has no family titles and no Lineage column, and the Base row comes after the fine-tunes. Not reproduced here: on the shared demo (one family, 4 files), within 400 ms of choosing By family the table showed 'Family from run demo-base · 2026-11-16 09:13 (4 files)' and a Lineage column, and it stayed so at 4 s (c3-models-byfamily.json). The cause is unknown. Inferred candidates: more runs or families, a slow answer, or a redraw lost in transit.
- **At ba6e85e**: Fix list rev 1 only, marked 'cause to be reproduced'. Not reproduced on the shared demo.
- **Done when**: After switching Order (including repeated and fast switches), the control, count line wording, family titles/Lineage column and row order all match the chosen order; while pending or failed the page says so.
- **Cause (reproduced in T02)**: the page's one listener (`_sync`, "always_last") reshaped the list only when the event that fired was a control, and Gradio 6.30 runs a deferred call again with the values gathered then but the first trigger's id. On a CPU demo copy at ba6e85e, with the selection's `_sync` join delayed by `page.route`: a row chosen, then By family, ended with the switch on By family, the count line "newest first", no family title, no Lineage. A fast By family then Newest first ended on By family: every answer wrote the controls back, so the late answer set the switch back and the deferred call read it. A Task chosen while a selection answered was dropped the same way.
- **Fix**: every answer shapes the view from the controls as they read and writes it when the list does not hold it (`same_view`, the view an input), whichever trigger asked; a control's value is written only where it cannot apply as it reads; a failed answer changes nothing and says under the options what was not applied (the error's kind only). D05: "Applying By family…" / "Applying Task: Disease…" beside the switch until the rows drawn say they answer the controls (`model_list.view_marker`, the view's `shaped_for`); no page lock.
- **Sources**: the owner's fix list rev 1 (2026-11-16)
- **Closed at**: 4e419fb (with d2d02f6 for the Task select's one request). Tests: `test_webui_models_page.py::test_a_control_changed_while_the_page_answers_is_applied_and_one_pick_is_one_request` (browser, the dashboard as mounted, one `_sync` answer held: a row then By family; By family then Newest first, the second answer held until the first one's rows are drawn; a row then Task Disease; each ends with the switch, the count line, the groups and Lineage agreeing with the last change and the line cleared, "Applying …" said while pending; on ba6e85e all fail: By family dropped, the switch set back, Disease dropped), and the unit cases `test_an_answer_writes_a_control_back_only_where_it_cannot_apply`, `test_an_answer_that_fails_says_what_was_not_applied_and_changes_nothing`, `test_the_rows_say_the_view_they_were_drawn_for_and_another_workspace_drops_it`. Seen on a CPU demo workspace at 1850×960 and 960×1080: the line under the switch at 14 px, gone once applied, no sideways scroll. The other "who fired" listeners were checked: no other always_last listener reads its trigger (the Diagnosis drawer's Task is a Radio whose one listener writes the view only).
- **Re-checked (verification, fcc3dea, part RB2)**: an Order switched before Models' opening answer arrived read By family over the newest-first rows for 0.6–1 s with no "Applying …" (V-RB2-5; under load): the line waited for rows carrying a view marker, and the rows the shared list draws before the page first shapes its view carry none. Fixed at 2972962: `PENDING_JS` reads unmarked rows as the list's own order, newest first, every task; test `test_webui_models_page.py::test_an_order_switched_before_the_first_answer_is_announced` (fails on fcc3dea).

### UI-06 Launch and configuration in the GUI

17 rows: 1 awaiting owner acceptance, 12 code-verified, 4 code-verified at ba6e85e (audit).

#### FL-SVC · Service stopped during manual acceptance with no recorded reason

- **State**: code-verified · found by other · page: shell
- **Problem**: The fix list's section 4 records that the manual-acceptance service log shows a normal shutdown at 16:09:14, with no crash trace and no known trigger. The reviewer restarted it with the acceptance launcher and handed it to a user-level background service. It is explicitly not a reproduced product crash, and the restart is not a fix for UI-06.
- **At ba6e85e**: Cause unknown, not reproduced. Recorded only in the fix list.
- **Progress (2026-11-16, T03 launcher)**: The stop asked for from Settings is named. `src/api/services/backend_control.stop_backend` (the Stop service backend path; the button is not built yet) logs `Stopping the service (pid N): asked for by Stop service in Settings.` before it sends the orderly stop's SIGTERM. Checked by test_backend_control (`test_stop_service_stops_a_real_server_through_its_orderly_stop`, a real uvicorn server): the line comes before the application's `Shutting down`. A stop by a signal from outside is still logged only as uvicorn's `Shutting down`, without the signal or its sender, and a crash writes no line, so the 16:09:14 shutdown would still read the same. A start without a terminal now appends the server's output to a launch log in the state directory, so such a log is kept at all (FL-UI-06).
- **Progress (2026-11-16, T03 gui-docs: b13e262)**: Stop service is in Settings beside Restart Backend: asked once ("Stop the service? …", Stop service now or Cancel), off while a GPU job runs (said beside it), locked with D8's reason for a read-only request, and an operator control checked on its own request (test_webui_data_locations, test_operator_access). In Chromium on the real launcher it stopped the server with the reason line in its log before uvicorn's `Shutting down`, and the instance file was removed. A stop by a signal from outside and a crash are still not named, as before.
- **Done when**: Every service stop writes a log line naming its trigger (signal, request, crash); a unit test sends SIGTERM and a stop request and checks the reason line.
- **Fix (T06 settings-service)**: the backend's shutdown-signal handlers hand the signal's number to the event loop, which logs it, with what usually sends it, before the application's shutdown (`main.stop_signal_words`): "Stopping the service (pid N): SIGTERM received, a stop from outside the service (a service manager, kill, a logout); who sent it is not known to the service." (SIGINT, SIGHUP, SIGQUIT and SIGBREAK have their own words; a second signal reads "received again while the service stops"). The SIGTERM Stop service sends itself says so ("sent by this service itself, asked for by Stop service in Settings", `backend_control.stop_asked_by`), after the request's own line. A crash, a SIGKILL or the out-of-memory killer can write no line: what it leaves is the start's instance info file, which only the orderly stop removes, so the next launcher start finding it with the lock free says so (`launch_instance.ended_without_stop_words`: "The previous start of this configuration (pid N, started T) ended without its orderly stop: it was killed (SIGKILL or the out-of-memory killer), crashed, was forced to quit, or the machine stopped; its last output is in <launch log>.").
- **Sources**: the owner's fix list rev 1 (2026-11-16)
- **Closed at**: 9dc4161. Tests: `test_backend_control.py::test_a_stop_signal_from_outside_is_named_in_the_log` (SIGTERM and SIGHUP sent by the test to a real uvicorn server: the line, before the application's shutdown), `::test_stop_service_stops_a_real_server_through_its_orderly_stop` (the request's line, then the signal's naming it the service's own), `test_service_unit.py::test_a_stale_info_file_with_a_free_lock_is_replaced` (the next start's warning, once); all four fail on the old code (a git archive of 455c1db with the tests copied in). Seen on a CPU demo service started from the worktree (scratch state): before, a SIGTERM from outside logged only uvicorn's "Shutting down"; after, the line above came first. The real launcher (`shep_launch.py --local-entry`, scratch HOME and XDG dirs) killed with SIGKILL and started again logged the warning with the earlier pid and start, then a SIGTERM was named and the info file removed. Limits, stated: a Python handler is not told the sender, so none is named; a server started by uvicorn alone (no launcher, so no info file) leaves no trace of a crash but its log's missing stop line; the fix list's 16:09:14 stop stays unexplained (its log is not here).

#### FL-UI-06 · Data locations and registry need environment variables before start; no GUI setup

- **State**: awaiting owner acceptance · found by the owner · severity unrated (fix list) · page: settings
- **Problem**: launch_shepherd.sh starts scripts/launch/shep_launch.py without loading any deployment file. src/config/server_settings.py reads M1, the workspaces root and the registry only from the environment and is read-only without M1. Settings saves compute settings only. A clinician therefore cannot open the dashboard by double click and set data locations in the GUI. The automated acceptance passed only because its script pre-set them.
- **At ba6e85e**: Fix list rev 1 only. Not built. Earlier records treat deployment settings as environment-only (M5-1 rule cited in the fix list).
- **Progress (2026-11-16, T03 core: dd5f162, f92676b, 3d27037)**: The owner withdrew the M5-1 environment-only rule. The service side is built: `server_settings` resolves every data location at its one read point (`_resolve`): an explicit environment variable first, then the applied values of a saved settings file outside the repository (`src/config/deployment_settings.py`: `SHEPHERD_SETTINGS_FILE`, else the user config dir), then nothing. Each `Setting` carries its `source`; a saved value is checked by the same directory rules at each read; an unreadable file is a problem naming it for every setting it would give, never read as empty. Writes go through `src/utils/settings_store.py` (the file's lock, one atomic writer shared with `runtime_presets.save_runtime_settings`, 0600/0700, an unreadable file renamed only by the explicit repair). Apply (`src/api/services/data_locations.py`) runs only with the job runner's slot idle and every GPU lock the old values name free; otherwise the change stays pending with its reason. The backend's startup tries it; `take_gpu_lock` re-reads the location once it holds the lock. The deployment mode gets the local start's default (`SHEPHERD_LOCAL_ENTRY=1` with a loopback bind host), and an explicit mode is never upgraded. The problem words point to Settings › Data locations, with the variable as "Ref:". Tests, the probe and its servers each name a settings file of their own. Checked by unit tests (test_server_settings, test_backend_control, test_operator_access, test_runtime_presets, test_probe_m5_acceptance, test_results_export). The real launcher was also started with `HOME` and `XDG_*` in scratch directories and no `SHEPHERD_*` set, after a save and apply from a plain shell. The Datasets page listed the demo workspace from the saved file at 1850x960, read-only. Started again with `SHEPHERD_LOCAL_ENTRY=1` set by hand (the launcher's `--local-entry` is not built), the same values were in effect and the header read 'Operator controls: on (this machine)'. A gated route answered 200 on loopback and 403 with a foreign Host or a forwarding header. Not built yet: the launcher's `--local-entry` and `--settings-file` and its any-cwd start, the Settings › Data locations panel, the HTTP view and CLI, and the docs. The double-click acceptance is not checked.
- **Progress (2026-11-16, T03 launcher)**: The launcher half is built. `launch_shepherd.sh` finds its own checkout (links followed) and runs that checkout's venv and launcher with `--local-entry`. It no longer looks for `.venv` in the directory it was started from. `launch_shepherd.cmd` does the same with `cd /d` to its folder (not run on Windows here). `shep_launch.py` changes to the repository before it starts the server, after it has read a relative `--settings-file` against the start directory. It removes its own variables from the environment it was given. It sets `SHEPHERD_LOCAL_ENTRY=1` only for `--local-entry` with a loopback bind, and `--settings-file` becomes the service's `SHEPHERD_SETTINGS_FILE`. One backend per configuration (`src/utils/launch_instance.py`): the launcher takes a lock named after the repository and the settings file in the user state directory and hands it through its exec to the server. It writes a 0600 info file (pid, port, URL, a random instance id, repository, settings file, log). `/health` answers that id, and the server removes the file at shutdown. A second start of a configuration whose lock is held and whose `/health` answers with the file's id opens that page and exits 0; a 200 without the id is never taken for it. Port: with no `--port`, 8000, else the first free one of 8001…8019, said in the log; a `--port` in use is refused with the reason. Without a terminal, `--local-entry` appends the launcher's and the server's output to `logs/launch.log` in the state directory, moved aside past 10 MB. Output already sent to a file or a pipe (`nohup … > my.log`, a script that reads it) stays there. A failure before the server is up (no venv, a refused bind, a refused port, a server that ends before it answers) opens a local error page saying what failed, what to do and where the log is. `launch_shepherd.sh --install-desktop-entry [--settings-file PATH]` writes `~/.local/share/applications/shepherd-advanced.desktop` (Exec runs this checkout's absolute `launch_shepherd.sh` by bash, `Terminal=false`, the repository's icon; a slot's entry has its own name). The Stop service backend path is in `backend_control.stop_backend`: refused while a GPU job or a model load or stop runs; after its delay it closes the idle slot and sends the orderly stop's SIGTERM. Its Settings button is not built. The backend's startup Apply was already wired by T03 core. Checked by unit tests (test_service_unit, test_launcher_browser_wait, test_launch_instance, test_backend_control) and the related files. With the real launcher, `HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME`/`XDG_DATA_HOME` in empty scratch directories and no `SHEPHERD_*` set: `launch_shepherd.sh` started with no terminal from another directory served from the repository. `/health` named the info file's id. With stdout at /dev/null the output was in the launch log, and with stdout in a file it stayed in that file. tests/integration/test_backend_stops_its_job.py passed (17), its `script` case included; that case first failed on the redirect, which was then narrowed. The operator controls were on (local start), and the demo workspace was listed from the saved file. A second start, and a start through the installed desktop entry's Exec line from `/`, opened the running page and exited 0. A slot with its own settings file on the taken port was refused with the error page. After a SIGTERM by pid the info file was gone and the lock free. The desktop Exec line started again on another port with the same saved values in effect (source saved). An explicit `SHEPHERD_DEPLOYMENT_MODE=read-only` start stayed read-only. A server that exited before answering opened the error page. Not done: the Settings › Data locations panel, the Stop service button, the HTTP view and CLI, and the docs. Not checked: a real desktop session's double click (no desktop here; the Exec line was run as a desktop runs it), Windows and macOS, and Restart Backend on a launcher-started server (the lock descriptor was seen without close-on-exec in the running server, and a unit test shows the lock goes through an exec).
- **Progress (2026-11-16, T03 gui-docs: e45d972, b13e262, c42a088, a81e83c, bcbf9ac)**: The GUI half is built. Settings has a Data locations panel beside Runtime settings (wide 55:45, narrow above it; `src/webui/components/data_locations.py`), drawn at each opening from one view of the data locations (`src/utils/deployment_view.py`, through `server_settings`' read point, no reader of its own). Each row (Workspaces folder, Results registry, Import folder, Training evidence folders) says what needs it, what is in effect and its source in plain words, the saved value when it differs, and a path box with Check (the same rule a job's read applies, the resolved path and what the server's lists find: "1 workspace") and Save (saved and applied at once). A required folder that is not set offers Create suggested folder (`~/SHEPHERD-data/<name>`, created only on that click, 0700 for the registry and the import folder). A row the environment sets keeps its box and Save and says the saved value applies once the start-up setting is removed. A pending change has one notice with its reason and Apply; an unreadable file one notice with Replace (renamed, kept), Save off and Create hidden. A read-only request sees "Set (path hidden)", every control locked and D8's reason once. Every act is an operator control, Check included. `GET /api/v1/settings/deployment` (open, read-only, paths hidden for a request D8 refuses) and `scripts/deployment_settings.py show` give the same view. The words that told a clinician to set `SHEPHERD_*` variables (the GPU chip and its bubble, the D8 chip's bubble, Datasets' refused root, Diagnosis' missing registry, the Test page's help) now point to Settings › Data locations, the variable only as "Ref:". Guides en and zh (§4.9, §4.11–§4.14), README, the playbook, PLAN_TEST_RESULTS' current rules and BACKLOG 45/17 say how it is set up now. Checked by unit tests (test_webui_data_locations, test_operator_access's gated handlers and route, test_server_settings' view and CLI, test_webui_gradio6, test_webui_chips, test_entry_documents, test_backend_control) and in Chromium on the real launcher. `launch_shepherd.sh` was started from another directory with `HOME` and `XDG_*` in empty scratch directories and no `SHEPHERD_*` set. The dashboard opened read-write (local start), with Settings showing "Nothing is set yet" and the GPU chip "refused, no workspaces folder". Create suggested folder made `~/SHEPHERD-data/registry` 0700 and saved it. Check on a folder holding the setup_demo.py workspace read "Usable … (1 workspace)", and Save read "Saved and in effect", the GPU chip turning to "none". Datasets then listed `demo`. The route answered the same view as `deployment_settings.py show` run from a plain shell (they differ only in the mode: the shell is not the local start). Stop service asked once, stopped the server with "Stopping the service (pid N): asked for by Stop service in Settings." in its log, and removed its instance file. Started again, the same values were in effect (Saved in Settings). A start with `SHEPHERD_DEPLOYMENT_MODE=read-only` showed "Set (path hidden)", every control locked with the reason, and no scratch path in the panel or the route. A start with `SHEPHERD_REGISTRY` set showed the override; a corrupted file showed the unreadable notice, and Replace kept the copy. At 1850x960 the page did not scroll and no text was under 14 px; at 760x1000 the panels stacked with no horizontal scroll. Screenshots outside the repository (scratchpad t03/gui-docs). With the scratch paths of about 120 characters, the overridden and unreadable states make the panel 850–870 px tall, so the Settings region scrolls by a few pixels at 1850x960; with paths like the mockup's they fit. Not checked: a double click in a real desktop session (no desktop here; the launcher part ran the desktop entry's Exec line), Windows and macOS, and the mockups' visual acceptance by the owner (not approved yet).
- **Progress (2026-11-16, T03 review fixes: 0f11217, 4e6fb0e)**: Two review findings fixed. (1) A read-only request saw a refused value's own words, which quote it as given: the redaction hides only absolute paths, so `SHEPHERD_REGISTRY='clinic/MRN 0012345/registry'` or `~/intake/Jane Doe` was shown by `GET /api/v1/settings/deployment` and the panel's Problem row. Hidden, a refused value, a location from an unusable settings file, a refused `SHEPHERD_SETTINGS_FILE` and the GPU lock under a refused folder now read in fixed words ("Results registry set when the service started is refused: an operator sees why in Settings › Data locations."); an operator still reads the refusal. (2) Saving and applying a folder that the service's own start-up setting overrides moved every other process's value: with `SHEPHERD_WORKSPACES_ROOT=A` on the service and B saved, a command run by hand with no variable took B's GPU lock while the service's job held A's. Apply now holds back such a key (`settings_store.held_back`): its applied value is kept and it applies at a start without the setting; a saved value equal to the setting applies as before. The panel offers no Apply for it, and its row's "Saved, applies when the start-up setting is removed" is now true. Checked by regression tests that fail on the old code (test_server_settings, test_webui_data_locations) and the related files (729 passed). The real launcher was then started with the override, a pending saved root and relative registry and import values, with `HOME`/`XDG_*` in scratch directories. Its startup Apply logged the hold-back and left the file's applied part empty. The read-only route showed no value, `held_back: ["workspaces_root"]` and the fixed words. A plain shell's `deployment_settings.py show` read "GPU lock: not configured". Not checked in a browser: the panel's held-back row (unit tests only).

It now says the reason from the check Apply makes, read without acting: the runner's slot (`TrainingManager.busy_reason`, the words `run_while_idle` refuses with), then every GPU lock the old values name (`settings_store.lock_refusal`, Apply's words); the service's view (`data_locations.view`) feeds the panel and `GET /api/v1/settings/deployment`, and Save's and Apply's lines point to the notice instead of repeating it. Every open (first start, second start, error page) now goes through `shep_launch.open_in_browser`: "Opened <url> in the browser." or "No browser could be opened on this computer: open <url> in a browser." when it answers False or raises. - **Progress (2026-11-16, T08 review fixes: 33664aa, b02d1d2)**: Two review findings on the T08 first-run fixes, each reproduced first. (1) The CPU line of setup_demo.py's last steps said to start the dashboard as `SHEPHERD_DEVICE=cpu bash launch_shepherd.sh`. On the first-run path the dashboard that saved the folder still runs, and a second start of the same configuration (`launch_instance.instance_key`: the repository and the settings file) only opens it, keeping its device. Seen with the real `shep_launch.py --local-entry`, scratch HOME and XDG dirs, port 9210, on a git archive of 5b838fd: first start with no device, folder saved through `data_locations.save_and_apply`, `setup_demo.py --train-model --device cpu`, then the advised start, which printed "already runs for this configuration" and exit 0; the running server's environment had no SHEPHERD_DEVICE, the Load's request (`POST /api/v1/pipeline/reload`, device left null as Diagnosis › Load a model… sends it) was refused ("Serving device 'auto' requires CUDA"), and the card read "Load refused" (`before_diagnosis-after-old-advice_*`). The line (`setup_demo.CPU_DASHBOARD`) now says a running dashboard keeps the device it was started with and a second start only opens it, so stop it first (Settings › Stop service), then start it with SHEPHERD_DEVICE=cpu; the guides (en, zh) say the same. Followed on the worktree, port 9211: Settings › Stop service in the browser (the log: "Stopping the service (pid N): asked for by Stop service in Settings."), the start with SHEPHERD_DEVICE=cpu, the same Load succeeded on cpu and the card read "Serving · Disease task model · demo-disease" (`after_diagnosis-after-new-advice_*`, `after_settings-stop-service_1850x960`). (2) The no-folder refusal quoted the setting's words, which end in their trailing Ref, and ran on after it ("No default is assumed. Ref: SHEPHERD_WORKSPACES_ROOT Or name the folder for this demo with --output-dir."); the GPU lock refusal (`--output-dir` given, no folder) did the same with "Nothing was written."; and "and there is none" was said of a folder that was set and refused (SHEPHERD_WORKSPACES_ROOT=/nonexistent). Both refusals now go through `setup_demo.refusal`: the lead, the setting's plain words (unset, or refused and why), `--output-dir`, "Nothing was written.", then the one Ref, split off by `operator_access.reason_parts` as D8's refusals do. Tests (each fails on a git archive of 5b838fd with the tests copied in, 9 failures): `test_setup_demo.py::test_with_no_workspaces_folder_in_effect_the_default_is_refused` (unset and refused, with and without --train-model; the whole printed line pinned), `::test_train_model_is_refused_before_anything_is_written[not configured]` (the line ends "Nothing was written. Ref: SHEPHERD_WORKSPACES_ROOT"), `::test_a_cpu_demo_says_to_stop_the_running_dashboard_before_its_cpu_start` (saved, set for the command, elsewhere), `::test_a_trained_demo_leads_with_the_dashboard_and_keeps_uvicorn_as_a_ref[cpu]`; `test_service_unit.py::test_a_second_start_of_a_running_configuration_opens_it` now gives the second start SHEPHERD_DEVICE=cpu (it passes on both: it pins the launcher fact the advice relies on). Related files passed (547), lint-imports kept. Before and after outputs rendered at 1850x960 and 760x1000 (`*_setup-demo-output_*`); logs and shots outside the repository (scratchpad t08fix/fixer). Not changed: `train_model.py`'s GPU lock refusal still prints "... Ref: SHEPHERD_WORKSPACES_ROOT Nothing was written." (the same glue in another command, whose present form `test_settings_projection.py` and `test_probe_m5_acceptance.py` pin and whose output the job cards show; left for a change of its own); the launcher's second start still says nothing of a start-up variable it does not apply. Not checked: Windows (`launch_shepherd.cmd` with the variable), a desktop session's real browser.
- **Done when**: Starting by double click with no environment variables opens the dashboard; Settings lets the operator set workspaces root and registry, saves them, and reuses them after restart; GUI, CLI and jobs read the same settings source; a missing location disables only the operations that need it.

#### R-12 · Restart Backend is one click with no confirmation and not tied to a pending restart

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity medium · page: settings
- **Problem**: Restart Backend was one click for every session; the page never showed running vs saved allocator.
- **At ba6e85e**: Running against saved is fixed. 'Allocator now' reads the allocator this backend runs from its own environment (running_allocator), and 'Allocator saved' is shown apart from it. When the two differ, the saved row shows a ▲ 'Not running yet: Restart Backend applies it', and this survives a reload because it is computed on every page open (test_webui_gradio6.py:156). In the demo both rows read 'Driver memory pool (cuda_async)'. The restart half is not fixed. An operator's click on Restart Backend still calls restart_backend() at once, with no confirmation step. The button is enabled whether or not a restart is pending, and it sits in the same row and at the same height (40 px) as Apply; only its variant (secondary) differs. One click still reconnects every open page. Read-only sessions are now locked out by D8.
- **Done when**: Clicking Restart Backend shows a confirmation row ('every open page reconnects' with Restart and Cancel), and Restart is enabled or marked as wanted only when the saved allocator differs from the running one.
- **Fix**: Restart Backend asks in the panel's one question row, shared with Stop service (`runtime_settings._ask`): "Restart the backend? Every open page reconnects in a few seconds. A loaded model is stopped first (…) and is not loaded again unless the service's start-up configures one.", with "Restart now" and Cancel; nothing is asked of the backend before Restart now. It is on only while a restart would apply a saved allocator (`restart_applies`: the saved preset is not the one running and no launch-environment setting overrides it, the rule the facts' "Not running yet: Restart Backend applies it" reads); otherwise off, with "Restart Backend is off: a restart would run the same allocator." beside it. Apply reads the buttons again. Stop service keeps its question, in the same row.
- **Sources**: UX:214 (appendix 12); RJ audits[0].findings[19]; UX:125 ('running against saved allocator')
- **Closed at**: 47a8c2e. Tests: `test_webui_gradio6.py::test_settings_asks_before_a_restart_and_says_each_fact_once_in_a_browser` (browser, the dashboard as mounted: Restart clicked, the question shown with Restart now and not Stop's button, Cancel closes it, asked again and confirmed; the backend asked once, by Restart now; the running allocator saved again with Apply turns Restart off with its reason, Stop service on), `::test_selecting_runtime_settings_refreshes_the_restart_lock`, `test_backend_control.py::test_the_restart_words_say_a_loaded_model_is_stopped_and_not_loaded_again`, `test_webui_data_locations.py::test_stop_service_asks_once_then_stops_through_the_orderly_stop`, and `test_operator_access.py`'s gated inventory (the question is an operator control). On the old code (a git archive of 455c1db, these tests copied in) 7 fail. Seen on a CPU demo service from the worktree (scratch HOME/XDG, M1) at 1850×960 and 760×1000: before, Restart was on with nothing waiting and one click restarted the backend at once ("Restarting backend" in its log, uptime back to 0, `before_restart-click_1850x960`); after, it is off with its reason while nothing waits, and with Expandable segments saved the click shows the question row (`after_restart-question_*`), Cancel closes it, and Restart now re-execs the backend, which then runs `PYTORCH_ALLOC_CONF=backend:native,expandable_segments:True`. No page scroll at 1850×960; the narrow page scrolls vertically only.
- **Review residual (T06-A, 2026-11-16)**: "Restart now" checked D8 only, so a question asked while another allocator was saved still re-exec'd the backend after the running allocator had been saved again (Apply on the page, or another tab and the slow timer) or a GPU job had started: every open page reconnected and a loaded model was stopped for a restart the page itself called off. Seen on a launcher-started server from a git archive of 373b962 (scratch HOME/XDG, M1): Restart off with "Restart Backend is off: a restart would run the same allocator." beside it and the question row still open; Restart now read "Restarting the backend…" and uptime went from 27.5 s to 0.5 s (`before_restart-stale-question_*`, `before_restart-now-clicked_*`). Fixed at the shared check: the question and the confirmed restart make one check (`runtime_settings._restart_refusal`: D8 on the event's own request, a GPU job, `restart_applies`), so Restart now asks nothing of the backend once Restart is off, and the row closes with the reason. Closed at cb0c9f7. Tests: `test_webui_gradio6.py::test_a_restart_question_left_open_restarts_nothing_once_restart_is_off` (the running allocator saved again, a job started, a restart still wanted) and the browser test `::test_settings_asks_before_a_restart_and_says_each_fact_once_in_a_browser` (the question left open, the running allocator applied, Restart now clicked: the reason shown, the row closed, the backend asked once in all); both fail on the old code. Seen on the worktree's launcher-started server at 1850×960 and 760×1000: Restart now read "○ Restart Backend is off: a restart would run the same allocator.", the row closed, uptime kept rising (6 s to 26 s) and the log has no "Restarting backend" line (`after_restart-now-clicked_*`; screenshots in scratchpad t06a/fixer-1). Not done (the review's optional part): Apply and the slow timer do not close an open question row when Restart turns off; it stays until Restart now (which now refuses) or Cancel.
- **Residual closed (T06-D1)**: Apply and the slow timer close a Restart question left open once Restart is off, through the same check (`runtime_settings._restart_question_closed` → `_restart_refusal`); a Stop question, a closed row and a Restart still wanted are left as they are; the reason is said once, beside Restart (the status line stays empty). Closed at f558ce7 and c4f5525. Tests: `test_webui_gradio6.py::test_an_open_restart_question_closes_once_restart_turns_off` (Apply and the timer; idle and busy) and the browser test `::test_settings_asks_before_a_restart_and_says_each_fact_once_in_a_browser` (the running allocator saved by another hand with the question open, then through Apply: the row closes, the reason beside Restart only); both fail on b68b56e. Seen on the dashboard as served by the test fixture at 1850×960 and 760×1000: before, the question and Restart now stayed under a disabled Restart; after, the row is gone (`before_/after_restart-question-after-allocator-saved_*`, scratchpad t06d1/api-races/shots). Seen, left: the facts above ("Allocator saved … ▲ Not running yet: Restart Backend applies it") are read when the page opens and after Apply, so a save from another tab leaves them saying a restart is wanted while Restart is off until the page is opened again. Found too, recorded under B46-mount: a Settings click made before Diagnosis' mount-time select leaves the session's location 'clinical', so the slow timer reads nothing on Settings.

Research › Training, where the demo is listed, was not named. With `--train-model` the output already led with the dashboard (T08 first run, b39bd3f).

FL-UI-06's Done when (a double-click start, Settings, one settings source) does not cover these lines, so the finding is a row of its own.

A test of the no-model output finds the dashboard first and fails on e0e4237.

#### T08-flow-9 · The launcher's read-only line continues in lower case after a full stop

- **State**: code-verified · found by the T08 browser flow · page: launch
- **Problem**: The launcher printed "Operator controls     : off. this deployment is read-only: …".
- **At 71587aa**: `scripts/launch/shep_launch.py` (~1097-1099) cut the shared reason's lead ("Operator controls are off, so ") off the sentence `server_settings.deployment_mode` gives and printed the rest after its own "off.". Seen in scratchpad/t08/A/logs/s3-run2-readonly.log.
- **Done when**: For each mode case (read-only set at start, M1 set at start, the local default) the line is whole, capitalised sentences, composed from where the shared words keep the reason; a test fails on e0e4237.
- **Fix**: `server_settings` keeps the reason in parts: `MODE_OFF_STATE` ("off, so this deployment is read-only"), then why in whole sentences (`MODE_NOT_SET_WHY`, `MODE_NOT_M1_WHY`); `MODE_NOT_SET` and `MODE_NOT_M1` are composed from them, and `mode_off_why(mode)` gives a mode's why, told apart by `unset`. The launcher prints "Operator controls     : off, so this deployment is read-only. The deployment mode set at start is not the operator mode. …". MODE_NOT_SET's words are unchanged; MODE_NOT_M1's second clause is now its own sentence (": the deployment mode" became ". The deployment mode") wherever D8 says it: on a deployment started with a mode that is not M1, the read-only chip's ⓘ ("For this request: …"), each refused control's "Refused: … Nothing was done." and the API's refusal. The launcher's other summary lines (Saved settings file, Single instance, Listening on, Access points, its warnings) print whole texts and have no such cut.
- **Sources**: the T08 browser flow (scratchpad/t08/A/logs/s3-run2-readonly.log)
- **Closed at**: 8afe2c7. - **Closed at**: b618863. The sequence is `atomic_json.write_text_atomic` (`write_json_atomic` serialises and calls it); the launcher's two writes call it with their modes (0600; 0644 in a 0755 directory). Test: `test_launcher_browser_wait.py::test_the_launchers_own_files_are_written_whole_by_the_one_atomic_writer` (fails on fcc3dea: the link's target overwritten).

#### RC-C · Diagnosis model drawer list and details share one scroll

- **State**: code-verified · found by the owner's recheck · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: In the Diagnosis model drawer reading the details moved the list.
- **At ba6e85e**: The list and the facts share one scroll container (.shep-model-body, overflow:auto). At 1850 x 960, with the first row chosen, the facts are 782 px tall inside a 566 px body. Scrolling the body 216 px to read the facts moved the list rows by the same 216 px (y 266 → 50). Load stays in reach: it is in the foot at y 859, outside the scroller.
- **Done when**: At 1850x960 with a row chosen, scrolling the details pane leaves the list rows at the same y positions (each has its own bounded scroll) and Load stays visible.
- **Sources**: UX:137-139; BL:1010 (item 52 (C)); BL:182
- **Closed at**: db16eea. Side by side (the shell's one layout decision, `style.SIDE`) the drawer's list and the chosen model's details are two regions, each its own scroll (`overflow: auto`, `overscroll-behavior: contain`) and its own minimum width (`--shep-panel-min`: the tree whole from 616 px, the details from 222 px; their sum fits the candidates and details panels the drawer covers, so wherever the page is side by side both fit), the body around them no scroll region, Load in the foot under both. Stacked (`style.STACK`, no media query) the page flows: the list, then the details, then Load; a row chosen brings its details' title to the top of the window and focuses it, and "↑ Back to the list" brings the chosen row back into view and focuses it (`model_list.FACTS_JS`, waiting for the facts that name the row). The shared tree's cells take 7 px a side in the shared style sheet (Models' own rule before), so the tree is whole in its region from 1240 px; the foot's note wraps beside Load instead of running past the drawer's edge. Measured on the multi-family demo (the demo-disease model served), a file of demo-b-disease chosen, the wheel over the details: at 7815893 one region (`.shep-model-body`, 503 px for 1171) and the list's first rows moved from y 364 to -303 at 1850x960 (from 384 to -569 at 1280x900); at db16eea the details scroll on their own (148 px at 1850x960, 717 at 1280x900), the list's rows stay at y 372/417/462/507 and Load at 861-899 (798-837 at 1280x900), nothing over it; stacked at 760x1000 and 390x844 no region scrolls inside, the chosen file's details title is at y 0 and focused (at 7815893 its details stayed 438 px below the window), Back to the list puts the row at y 462-507 (383-428), focused. Tests: tests/unit/test_webui_model_list.py::test_the_drawers_list_and_details_each_scroll_on_their_own_in_a_browser (side by side the list stays while the details scroll, Load in view; at 1280x900 nothing cut or scrolled sideways; stacked the order, the focus and the return; fails on 7815893), tests/unit/test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_the_load_drawers_list_and_details_are_two_regions_that_fit_side_by_side (the regions, their minimums summed against the panels, no media query; fails on 7815893), and the shared check tests/unit/test_webui_shell.py::test_every_page_at_every_size_passes_the_ui_measurement_check, which now opens the drawer at 1850x960 as well as 1280x900, 1366x620 and 390x844 and chooses a row (`browser.UI_WALK_JS` `pick`), an area a page marks inside a panel (`shep-area`) counted as its own: no drawer finding; its one finding, a Settings data-locations box under the runtime footer at 1366x620, is there at 7815893 too (not this row's). Screens: scratchpad/t05/drawer-rows/shots2/before_drawer_details_scrolled_1850x960.png, scratchpad/t05/drawer-rows/shots2/after_drawer_details_scrolled_1850x960.png (and _1280x900, _760x1000, _390x844), after_drawer_select_*, after_drawer_back_to_list_760x1000.png and _390x844.png; measurements scratchpad/t05/drawer-rows/shots2/before_drawer.json, scratchpad/t05/drawer-rows/shots2/after_drawer.json.

#### R-49 · Continue task split across drawer and run bar with duplicate controls

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity medium · page: training
- **Problem**: Continue split one task across the drawer and run bar, with duplicate controls.
- **At ba6e85e**: Continue Training (or Start branch) and Cancel are now in the drawer's footer beside their reason (7649fa0; seen), and Continue… and Stop are hidden in the run bar while the drawer is open. Still split across two regions: the run bar keeps a disabled Start with 'Start disabled: close Continue… to start a new run.' and the line 'RUN a new one, continued from the checkpoint chosen in Continue or branch a run'. The drawer's × Close and the footer's Cancel do the same thing (training_console.py:4715-4717). The 'Checkpoint source: From run list / Manual path' radio still takes a row near the top.
- **Done when**: While the Continue drawer is open, the run bar shows no disabled Start, no Start reason and no 'RUN a new one' line; the drawer has one closer and 'Manual path' sits behind a link.
- **Integration (T05 merge, 2026-11-17)**: after the merge, `test_the_continue_drawer_and_the_fine_tune_form_fit_the_owners_window` failed 2 runs of 3 with the run bar's reason read as "1.5s". That text is Gradio's status tracker inside the reason's block (captured as `<div data-testid="status-tracker" …>`), showing a queued render's elapsed time under load, although every event that writes the reason hides its progress (the served config lists none with `show_progress` other than hidden, and Gradio's tracker ignores hidden only for a validation error). The page's own reason (`.shep-rb-reason`) was empty each time. The walk now reads that element (b7f0b34's follow-up); the brief tracker under a heavy load is Gradio's and is recorded, not changed.
- **Sources**: UX:251 (appendix 49); RJ audits[1].findings[7]; UX:130 (section 4: Continue's buttons in the drawer's footer)
- **Closed at**: 92084f4. While the drawer is open the run bar draws no Start, no reason and no 'RUN a new one' line (`run_bar_view` clears the reason; 'Start disabled: close Continue… to start a new run.' and the `shep-train-continues` line, its listener and CSS are removed). The drawer has one closer, 'Close' in its header (`shep-train-drawer-close`; the footer's Cancel is removed). The checkpoint source radio is hidden (`visible="hidden"`) and chosen by a link beside the server-list note, 'Type a path instead ›' / 'Choose from the list instead ›' (`SOURCE_LINKS`, `source_link_html`, `_on_source_link`). Tests: `test_training_page.py::test_with_the_drawer_open_the_run_bar_draws_no_action_and_the_drawer_one_closer` (fails on b2732ea) and the shared walk's drawer stop, `test_webui_shell.py::test_the_continue_drawer_and_the_fine_tune_form_fit_the_owners_window`: on b2732ea it reports the run bar's actions ['shep-train-start']; after, none and one closer at 1850x960 and 960x1080. Close draws a 3 px ring (outline solid 3px rgb(67, 74, 88) on :focus-visible); the walk's one Tab press had focused it, the drawer's first control, so the walk read its look before focus while it already had focus, and the cue changed nothing. The walk now reads each control's look unfocused (`browser.focusWalk` blurs a control that holds focus first; the 3:1 rule unchanged). Tests: `test_webui_shell.py::test_the_focus_walk_reads_a_controls_cue_from_it_unfocused` (a static page; on 54d5f4b's fixture the first ringed button reads '#first "First" none') and the shared check, which passes at every stop, both drawer stops included (`test_webui_shell.py`: 45 passed; at 54d5f4b: 1 failed, the drawer's Close).
