### UI-09 R10 train/test overlap presentation

2 rows: 1 code-verified, 1 code-verified at ba6e85e (audit).

#### R-63 · R10 train/test overlap buried in two run-on dot-joined paragraphs

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity high · page: test
- **Problem**: The R10 overlap was two neutral paragraphs.
- **At ba6e85e**: R10 is still two key-value rows. Each holds one run-on sentence joined with ' · ' and keeps the stutter. For example: 'inputs (evidence found): Training disease overlap: overlap — 3 of 5 distinct test diseases; 7 test cases with one of them · Training phenotype-set overlap: overlap — 2 of 10 test cases'. It is plain dark text (computed colour rgb(29,34,48)) with no mark or colour, although 3 of 5 test diseases were seen in training. There is no table. Models' recorded results show the same paragraphs.
- **Done when**: Test and Models show R10 as a table (training/validation x disease/phenotype set) with a status mark on each overlap cell and no repeated 'overlap: overlap' stutter.
- **Reproduced in T02**: on a CPU demo copy at the round's base (0f4ae91, these renderers unchanged since ba6e85e), a disease measurement of the demo's child on a supplied cohort with R10 evidence found read on Test and on Models 'R10, training inputs (evidence found): Training disease overlap: overlap — 3 of 5 distinct test diseases; 7 test cases with one of them · Training phenotype-set overlap: overlap — 2 of 10 test cases', no table and no mark.
- **Fix**: the one R10 rendering is a table (`renderers.r10_table_html`): a row per scope (Training, Validation) with its evidence state under it, a column per label (Disease overlap, Phenotype-set overlap), each cell its state by the shared mark and its count, denominator, unit and cases, or its unverifiable reason, read by the one reading of a label's state (`r10_state_parts`; `r10_state_text` joins it as before). An overlap is amber ▲ and blocks nothing; "none" keeps the caveats' words with its count and no green mark; an unverifiable cell has no number. The governed words are unchanged (skill §13, 2026-10-25). Test shows it under 'Train/test overlap (R10)' with the R10 labels' ⓘ; Models' record and Models › Results' fold show the same table.
- **Sources**: UX:265 (appendix 63); RJ audits[2].findings[2]; UX:68 (section 3 high #7); UX:71-72; UX:127; UX:153-157 (decision 3); BL:1010 (52); SK:63; SK:189-193
- **Closed at**: 751b8d7. Tests: `test_webui_models_page.py::test_a_records_r10_is_a_table_with_a_mark_on_each_overlap_and_no_stutter`, `::test_unverifiable_is_a_word_and_never_a_count`, `::test_a_records_r10_reads_in_its_states_words_never_a_count`, `test_webui_test_page.py::test_unverifiable_is_a_word_never_a_count`, `::test_a_kept_reports_r10_reads_in_its_states_words_never_a_count`, `::test_the_r10_help_sits_beside_the_r10_lines` (each fails on 0f4ae91: no R10 table). Seen on the CPU demo copy at 1850×960, 1536×864 and 960×1080: Test's Result and Models' record show the table, three overlap cells amber ▲ (rgb(122,70,0)) with '3 of 5 distinct test diseases; 7 test cases with one of them', '2 of 10 test cases', '1 of 5 …; 2 test cases …', the fourth 'None in the inputs this checkpoint records (not evidence about data outside them) 0 of 10 test cases' unmarked; no sideways page scroll; nested in Models' record the table's header is not sticky (computed position static).

#### OWN-1 · Pages still overloaded and hard to read (theme layer unchanged)

- **State**: partly done · found by the owner · severity overall · page: shell
- **Problem**: After M5's functional pass the owner said the pages are still overloaded and hard to read.
- **At ba6e85e**: The round improved the shared pieces: status marks, gated buttons, short options, summary lists, plain field labels and a footer that leads with the consequence. The theme layer that the review (UX §2) blamed for the overload remains: a flat type scale (four sizes and four weights, no 20 px title), invisible input borders (1.52:1 at most), PREPARE/EVALUATE on the tab row, a level-1 switch identical to the in-page radios, and inverted chip emphasis. Pages still carry engineering detail, for example Datasets' footnotes naming scripts/build_knowledge_graph.py, current_data_dir and workspace_build.json. No UX acceptance by the owner has happened since 2026-10-25.

### UI-11 One action, one operation

4 rows: 4 code-verified.

#### B46-races · Brief older displays on Training/Test pill, banner, Continue drawer and job card

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · severity non-blocking · page: training
- **Problem**: A tick crossing a render can show an older pill; banner dismissal; Continue drawer can show the previous workspace's list/check; Test job card briefly older.
- **At ba6e85e**: The demo has one workspace and no running job, so the brief older displays cannot be produced (a tick crossing a render on the pill, a banner dismissal, the Continue drawer's previous-workspace list or check, the Test job card). Per the record, the pill is still written by both the fast poll and the renders. The record keeps these as an open, non-blocking follow-up.
- **Done when**: With two workspaces and a running job, a test that crosses a poll tick with a render or switches workspace never shows an older pill, a re-shown dismissed banner, the previous workspace's Continue list/check, or an older Test job card.
- **T02**: not attempted. The pill's two writers (the fast poll and the renders, `POLL_KEYS`) are the Training page's documented render/poll contract; ending the race needs one writer or an ordering stamp there, and the reproduction needs two workspaces and a running job. The select-event fix of RJ-G6 does not change it.
- **Reproduced (T06-D1)**: through Gradio's own queue and routes (one session, the served page), each on the old wiring: a fast tick held in its read of the job (running) while the job ended and a render drew "Completed"; released, the tick's answer came last and put the "Running · epoch 1/4" pill back. Continue… opened on `demo` with its listing held while `resampled` was chosen and the drawer relisted: the relisting ran first (each in a group of its own; the drawer not yet open, it listed nothing) and the held listing was applied last, so the note read "listed demo" under a run bar on `resampled`. On Test, a slow tick held in its job read (running, elapsed 00:00:12) while an act's read drew "Completed": the tick's card came last. Dismiss wrote the banner itself outside the group (a render that crossed the click redrew it), found by the contract test, not driven in a browser.
- **Fix**: the render/poll contract in one place per page: the queued render in the selection's group is the one writer of every piece drawn from the selection or from a read of the job. Training: the fast poll writes no piece (`POLL_KEYS` removed; the pill is one of `HANDED_KEYS`, so a changed pill is handed over like any piece); Dismiss records the dismissal in the group and the render after it draws the banner; every listener that writes the session's selection (`ws`, `mode`, the run viewed's choice, the banners dismissed) or the Continue drawer's list, choice and check (open, Refresh, relist, run and checkpoint choice, source, operation, Check, the start, Branch edits, Stop's question and confirmation) runs in the run bar's group (`ctx.training_workspace_writes`). Test: the slow poll writes no card; a changed card is handed to `_job_card_now` in the selection's group, which reads the job at its turn. Cost: while a job runs a hand-off render follows every changed tick (the pill's and the card's elapsed time); a slow drawer step (a listing, a check) holds the group's other renders until it answers.
- **Sources**: BL:1004 (item 46)
- **Closed at**: ea25f1b. Tests: `test_training_page.py::test_every_writer_of_the_selection_or_a_piece_drawn_from_it_is_in_the_group` (contract: on the old wiring the poll's pill, Dismiss and every drawer and Stop listener were outside the group), `::test_a_tick_that_crosses_a_render_never_brings_back_an_older_pill` and `::test_a_workspace_chosen_while_the_drawer_lists_never_shows_the_previous_list` (served, Gradio's queue: the held tick and the held listing above), `test_webui_test_page.py::test_the_job_card_has_one_kind_of_writer_the_selections_group` and `::test_a_tick_that_crosses_an_acts_read_never_brings_back_an_older_job_card` (served); each fails on 5357267 and passes now. Related files: 1204 passed (Training, Test, Fine-tune, shell, D8, probe, Datasets). Not seen in a browser window: the races are transient and were driven through the served queue, not by hand; no visual change to screenshot.

#### RJ-G6 · One dropdown pick sends its .input event twice

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity unrated (research principle) · page: shell
- **Problem**: Research G6 recorded from Gradio 6.30's source, not yet confirmed in a browser, that an option pick dispatches 'input' and then blur dispatches it again. It named the repo's .input wiring on dropdowns, and R-61's fix asked to 'switch model.input to .change or .select (G6)'. Reproduced now on the shared server by counting queue joins. One pick on Test's Run select sent shep-test-model-run.input twice, and one pick on Training's Viewing select sent shep-train-view-run.input twice (c2-dropdown-events.json). The wiring is unchanged: test_page.py:3289, 3313, 3342, 3344, 3362; training_console.py:4651, 4657; finetune_page.py:1316; model_list.py:1781.
- **At ba6e85e**: Recorded only in review.json's research. No later record or test names it. Reproduced at ba6e85e.
- **Done when**: A browser test counting queue joins shows exactly one request per option pick on Test's Run select and every other dropdown wired with .input.
- **Cause (measured in T02 on a bare Gradio 6.30 page)**: a single-select Dropdown sends `input` on every blur and once more for an option clicked (2 per pointer pick, 1 per keyboard pick, 1 for a focus that chose nothing). Its `select` is not sent for a pick made with the arrow keys and Enter, and `change` follows the server's own writes too (Test writes its selects back on purpose), so neither is the choice's event; its `blur` comes once per option chosen and never from a write. A multiselect and a Radio send `input` once per change.
- **Fix**: `shell.picked(control)`, the one place that names a choice's event (a single-select Dropdown's blur, any other control's input), wired at every select choice: the shared list's workspace, Models' Task, Test's workspace, version, checkpoint and run, Training's dataset and Viewing, Fine-tune's dataset, Diagnosis' reference cohort, the Results segment's filters. No debounce; two real picks are two requests.
- **Sources**: docs/working/webui-ux-review-2026-10-25/review.json research[1].principles[5] (G6-dropdown-events); docs/working/webui-ux-review-2026-10-25/review.json audits[2].findings[0] (fix: 'Also switch model.input to .change or .select (G6)')
- **Closed at**: d2d02f6. Tests: `test_webui_shell.py::test_every_select_choice_in_the_dashboard_is_its_one_event_per_pick` (the whole dashboard: no single-select Dropdown choice on `input`; the named selects on blur), `test_webui_shell.py::test_one_pick_of_a_select_is_one_request_and_the_pages_write_is_none` (browser, Gradio's own page: pointer 1, keys 1, the server's write 0, a Radio pick 1), and the Models browser test above (one Task pick by pointer and by keys: 1 request each; 2 for the pointer pick on ba6e85e). Seen in the dashboard on a CPU demo workspace, counting queue joins: Test's Run select and Training's Viewing select, 1 request per pointer pick and 1 per keyboard pick (2 and 1 on ba6e85e). The training touch record (Textbox, Number, Checkbox, Radio) is unchanged.

### UI-12 What bound and verified mean

3 rows: 2 code-verified, 1 code-verified at ba6e85e (audit).

#### B72-T3 · Tampered pairs.bin or split record still listed as 'Bound', refused only at Start

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · page: test
- **Problem**: list_versions reads cohort.json alone.
- **At ba6e85e**: list_versions still reads cohort.json and nothing else. Reproduced on my isolated copy: I changed one byte of demo-kg-1's pairs.bin. The version still read '● Bound to this graph' and Start was enabled with no reason. The run then failed at start: 'measure_scorer: refused: …pairs.bin is not the pairs file … cohort.json binds (its bytes are c35c7d4a53b1..., the version binds 28e2b12861ba...)'. The table got a grey 'Failed' row. (I restored the byte afterwards.)
- **Done when**: After changing one byte of a version's pairs.bin (or contradicting its split record), the version list no longer reads 'Bound to this graph' and Start is disabled with a reason naming the mismatch.
- **Reproduced in T02**: on a copy of a CPU demo registry (0f4ae91, listing unchanged since ba6e85e), one byte of demo-kg-1's pairs.bin changed: `binding_state` 'bound', the Data panel '● Bound to this graph', Start enabled with no reason.
- **Fix**: `cohort_registry.kept_contents` checks the files a version keeps (pairs.bin, samples.json, a generated split's kept split manifest and fixture) by `check_references`' one comparison, now one function (`_located_state`), so a kept split manifest whose blocks contradict the version's copies differs too; it is remembered by the version's directory and cohort.json digest with each file's stat identity, so a listing that finds the files as they were hashes nothing (a file changed within 2 s of a check, missing or unreadable is checked again). The WebUI's listing asks for it (`list_cohort_versions(contents=True)`: Test, Datasets, the Diagnosis reference cohorts); the open route hashes nothing (its `contents` is null). `workspaces.binding_state` says the facts apart: 'changed' (a kept file other bytes or gone: never bound), 'bound' (the record names this graph and the files were checked; Test adds 'files checked <time>'), 'unknown' (the files cannot be checked now). The run's own content and fingerprint refusals are unchanged.
- **Sources**: BL:1030
- **Closed at**: 49d281e. Tests: `test_cohort_registry.py::test_a_version_whose_kept_file_changed_is_told_apart_by_its_bytes`, `::test_a_check_of_unchanged_files_is_answered_without_hashing_them`, `test_webui_test_page.py::test_a_version_whose_kept_file_changed_is_not_bound_and_start_says_why` (samples.json of a supplied cohort), `test_webui_datasets.py::test_the_versions_not_listed_under_a_workspace_are_counted_by_why`, `test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_the_reference_cohort_options_are_short_unique_and_keep_the_full_label` (each fails on 0f4ae91; the Datasets one lists the changed version as bound). Seen on the CPU demo copy at 1850×960, one byte of demo-kg-1's pairs.bin changed: Graph '✕ Not bound: its files changed after the import' (rgb(165,34,34)), under the list '✕ Not measured: the version's pairs.bin holds 436875cb1c34..., not the 28e2b12861ba... recorded. …', Start disabled with 'Disabled: the data version's files changed after its import (see Data).'; on the untouched registry '● Bound to this graph files checked <UTC time>' and Start enabled. A generated split's contradicted split record is covered by the shared comparison's own tests, not seen in the browser (the demo has no generated version).

#### B53 · Open read routes name server paths the pages hide

- **Problem**: pipeline/status and training/status return absolute paths, so the pages' redaction can be read around (API, not a page).
- **At ba6e85e**: Reproduced for a read-only request (X-Forwarded-For): GET /api/v1/training/status answers gpu.lock.path as the absolute server path (…/scratchpad/audit-ui/ws/.shepherd-gpu.lock). GET /api/v1/pipeline/status carries no path while no model is loaded; its current_data_dir and current_checkpoint_path need a served model to show.

- **Fix**: the decision is the rev 2 plan's (UI-13: no server path fragment to a request that is not allowed; an operator keeps what it needs). One function at the service boundary, `operator_access.answer_for`: a request D8 allows gets the answer as it is; any other the same JSON-ready answer with every absolute path in every text replaced by "a server path" (`path_redaction.redact_value`, the walk `run_environment.recorded_components` now uses too), the service's own `/api/v1/` routes named in a reason kept. Both status routes return through it; the pages read the same values in process and are unchanged.
- **Sources**: BL:1011 (item 53); OPS:364
- **Closed at**: ee6d0aa. Test: `test_operator_access.py::test_the_open_status_routes_name_no_server_path_to_a_request_d8_does_not_allow` (a workspaces root named `Jane (MRN 1) ws`, a served data directory and checkpoint: with X-Forwarded-For and from a non-loopback address both routes answer `gpu.lock.path`, `current_data_dir` and `current_checkpoint_path` as "a server path" and no part of the root; a loopback M1 request gets the paths; fails on 32a286e at the lock's path), with the API tests beside it (601 passed). Seen on the real service (CPU demo, M1, a disease model served): read-only, 0 absolute paths in either answer; operator, the lock path and both served paths. Left, outside this row: BACKLOG 53's `GET /workspaces` and `GET /cohorts` problem texts were not checked here.
- **Review residual (T02)**: the kept routes (`_ROUTES`, `/api/v1/`) were marked inside a path too, so a read-only request got a run directory '/srv/clinic/api/v1/Jane Doe MRN 1/runs/x' as 'a server path/api/v1/Jane Doe MRN 1/runs/x'. The same held for a directory root found inside a longer path ('/mnt/old/srv/ws/Jane Doe (MRN 1)/x.json' → 'a server path<workspaces>/Jane Doe (MRN 1)/x.json'). Fixed in the one pattern (`path_redaction._MARK`): a path that runs into a root's text is replaced whole, and a route in a reason is kept as before. Closed at 6f6b19e. Tests: `test_operator_access.py::test_the_open_status_routes_name_no_server_path_to_a_request_d8_does_not_allow[srv/api/v1/Jane (MRN 1) ws]`, `::test_an_open_answer_keeps_a_route_its_reason_names_and_no_part_of_a_path` (in a text, in quotes, in a key; 'POST /api/v1/pipeline/reload' kept) and `test_webui_test_page.py::test_a_roots_text_inside_a_longer_path_is_that_paths_own` (4 cases through both page redactions). Each fails on 0a0e300's pattern. 1035 passed across the redaction, API and page test files beside them. Not rechecked on a running service: the route test uses the real app through its test client.
- **Leftover checked (T06-D1)**: BACKLOG 53's `GET /workspaces` and `GET /cohorts`. Reproduced with a workspaces root and a registry whose entries' reasons name server paths (a malformed `split_manifest.json`, a workspace with none, a link resolving outside the root; a malformed `cohort.json`, a version with none), from a non-loopback address and with `X-Forwarded-For`: both lists answered every reason with its absolute path (5 and 2 paths in the answers, e.g. "…/Jane (MRN 1) ws/broken/split_manifest.json is not UTF-8 JSON …"); the other open GET routes answered none. Fixed at the shared root: both lists return through `answer_for`, and `answer_for` names the service's data locations to the one redaction as known directories replaced whole (`path_redaction.redact_paths`: a directory named None), since a root whose own name holds a space and ends a clause ("… outside the workspaces root …/Jane (MRN 1) ws, so …") left "(MRN 1) ws" under the pattern alone. An operator's request gets the lists as before. Closed at b68b56e. Tests: `test_operator_access.py::test_an_open_read_route_names_no_server_path_to_a_request_d8_does_not_allow[*]` (every open GET route without a path parameter, both read-only requests) with `::test_every_open_read_route_is_checked_for_server_paths` (the route list is the app's); `/workspaces` and `/cohorts` fail on 4d2ebda, and `/workspaces` fails with the lists wired but the data locations unnamed. Seen through the app's test client, not on a running service. Left: a path outside every data location whose last part holds a space (a link's target "…/elsewhere MRN 2, …") still ends at that space under the one pattern, on every surface alike (`path_redaction`'s documented rule).
- **Review residual (T06-D1 review)**: `_data_locations` named every configured text a hidden server directory, a refused relative one too, and the one redaction replaces such a text as a substring, an object's key included. With `SHEPHERD_WORKSPACES_ROOT=workspaces` a read-only `GET /api/v1/workspaces` answered `{"a server path": [], "problem": "The a server path folder … root 'a server path' is not an absolute path …"}` (the list key gone, the refusal unreadable); with `.` every ". " read "a server path"; `/cohorts` and the lock's problem in `/training/status` alike. A location of `/` (absolute, accepted) took every slash ("scriptsa server path", "/api/v1/pipelinea server pathreload"). Fixed in `_data_locations`: only an absolute path other than a filesystem root is a hidden directory (`_a_server_directory`), and the evidence folders' environment text is split by `os.pathsep` as its reader splits it, so a refused entry "…/Jane Doe ev" is replaced whole ("Doe ev" was left). Closed at 8f13b67. Tests: `test_operator_access.py::test_a_refused_relative_location_leaves_an_open_answers_keys_and_words[workspaces|.|data]` (a read-only request gets the operator's `/workspaces`, `/cohorts` and lock answer) and `::test_an_open_answer_hides_each_evidence_folder_and_never_a_root_s_slashes`; all four fail on cb9afc1. Seen through the app's test client (`api-answers-before.txt` / `api-answers-after.txt`, scratchpad t06d1/fixer-d1: 6 garbled answers before, 0 after). Not done (the review's optional part): a hidden root's match is not anchored on its left, since the one pattern's documented rule needs a root's text inside a longer path to join that path (`/mnt/old/srv/ws/…`); an absolute root such as `/data` still matches inside a relative path's text (`train/data`), none of which an open answer was seen to hold.

#### B72-N5 · Path outside every root with a bracketed spaced part is only partly redacted

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · severity minor · page: shell
- **Problem**: '/data/Jane (MRN 1)/x.json' shows its bracket on.

Test: `test_webui_test_page.py::test_a_bracketed_spaced_name_goes_whole_and_the_sentence_keeps_its_punctuation` (7 cases through the Test page's redaction and `workspaces.redact`: 'Cannot read /data/Jane (MRN 1)/x.json: denied' → 'Cannot read a path outside the workspaces root: denied'; a non-ASCII name with a bracket and a space; 'MRN 0012345.json:'; no 'MRN', 'Jane', 'x.json' or '患者' left; 6 of 7 fail on ba6e85e's pattern, the prose bracket passes on both), and the redaction tests beside it (80 passed). A worst-case text of 2,000 bracketed words is redacted in under 2 ms.

### UI-14 Reload restores the page and valid selections

4 rows: 1 outside this round's scope (owner to decide), 3 code-verified.

#### B32-UI8 · Every open page lands on Diagnosis after a reconnect reload

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · page: shell
- **Problem**: After a restart every page lands on Diagnosis; the chart view returns only when Training is opened (M5-3 review UI-8); also per-page links, pages by role, New/Continue merged recorded.
- **At ba6e85e**: Reproduced in a reload (the page-level part of a reconnect reload; the service was not restarted): with Research › Models open, the page came back on Diagnosis (clinical). The shell builds level 1 with selected=CLINICAL, and no page is kept in sessionStorage or in the address; the head script writes only __theme=light.
- **Done when**: With Research › Models open, a page reload (and a reconnect reload) comes back on Research › Models, with the outer and research page kept in sessionStorage or the address.
- **Fix**: the head script keeps the page shown (its id only: `clinical`, `settings` or a research page) for this browser tab in sessionStorage (`shell.KEPT_PAGE`), reads it before the first screen is drawn and keeps nothing until the shell has answered; the page load hands it to `shell._restore`, which opens a research page or Settings as a cross-link does (`PageContext.go`, so only that page refreshes) and leaves Diagnosis, nothing kept or an unknown value. No address, case data, path or choice is kept, and nothing is started, loaded or registered: the page opened reads its state as any opening does. Stable selections inside a page are not restored.
- **Sources**: BL:990 (item 32)

#### B46-mount · Diagnosis tab mount writes the session location whatever page is shown

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · page: shell
- **Problem**: About 1.5 s after load the location becomes Diagnosis, so a page opened before polls nothing.
- **At ba6e85e**: The code is unchanged: Diagnosis' tab select writes the session's location with no guard. Observed: on load, with no click, the page sends _shown_clinical (dependency 474) at about 1.4 s, along with Diagnosis' card refresh (dependency 16). The race itself was not reproduced in two tries: when Research and then Models were clicked at about 1.37 s, before that select, the Diagnosis select did not fire at all. Inferred to remain possible within a narrower timing window.
- **Done when**: A Diagnosis tab select fired at mount does not overwrite the session location when another page is already shown; a test opening Models before that select sees Models' polling continue.
- **Cause (reproduced in T02)**: Diagnosis' and Settings' selects wrote the location without the queue, beside the queued research selects; a mount-time select answered after Research › Models had been opened set the location back to Diagnosis. In Chromium, with that select answered 1.5 s late (a busy server), the location the server held after every answer was 'clinical' while Models was shown.
- **Fix**: every location writer (Diagnosis', Settings', Research's and each research tab's select) is one queued group of one, run in the order sent, each select sent once (`shell.LOCATION_WRITES`, `trigger_mode="multiple"`).
- **Sources**: BL:1004 (item 46, end)
- **Closed at**: 1c44b82. Tests: `test_webui_shell.py::test_diagnosis_mount_select_never_writes_over_a_page_opened_after_it` (browser: the mount-time select sent, then Research › Models opened, then, once every request is answered, the location the server holds, which is what Models' poll reads, is "models"; on ee6d0aa "clinical") and `test_the_location_follows_the_page_shown` (all eight writers queued in the group). Left: the order is the order the server receives the joins in, as for the other queued groups.
- **Residual seen (T06-D1, not fixed: outside this lane)**: on a dashboard served by the test fixture, Settings clicked as soon as its tab was there (1.5 s after load) sent `_shown_settings` at 2.30 s and Diagnosis' mount-time `_shown_clinical` was sent after it, at 2.69 s; both queued, in that order, so the location the server held was 'clinical' while Settings was shown, and the slow timer's Settings poll returned nothing (its two ticks read 'clinical'). The R-12 browser test waits for the mount-time select's answer before opening Settings. The fix here orders the writers as the server receives them; a mount-time select that arrives after a page chosen by hand still overwrites it.
- **Re-checked (verification, fcc3dea)**: the residual above happens after a reload too (a mount-time select sent after the restore had opened Training: V-6, P1). Fixed at 9b66b1a: every writer writes only for the page shown as it was sent and never over a later write (V-6).

### UI-15 Diagnosis input changes and result identity

3 rows: 3 code-verified.

#### B73 · Genes box edited without a keypress keeps the old diagnosis result

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: Select-all+Delete, cut or paste changes the genes while the old result stays (read from Gradio's code; not reproduced).
- **At ba6e85e**: Not reproduced; this is from the code. The genes box still clears the result only on .input (genes_input.input(**edited)), so an edit with no keypress keeps the old result. The HPO box uses .change. The demo serves no gene task model, so the genes box never appears here.
- **Done when**: A unit/UI test shows that changing the genes box by paste, cut or select-all+Delete (a .change event) clears the shown result, as the HPO box already does.
- **Reproduced (T02)**: in Chromium, the panel served with a gene task model's stand-in status and result: after a result, select all then Delete, a cut (Ctrl+X) and inserted text (an input method's or a paste's: an input event, no keypress) changed the genes and left the result; typing cleared it.
- **Fix**: the genes box clears on `.change`, as the HPO box does. No handler writes the box (the card shows it by its container's class), so a change is the user's edit or Clear; the cohort select keeps its pick listener. The Training touch record is not used.
- **Sources**: BL:1031 (item 73); OPS:371
- **Closed at**: 620056f. Tests: `test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_every_edit_of_the_genes_box_clears_the_result_shown` (browser, the real panel and shell: each of the four edits clears the result, "Inputs changed"; on ee6d0aa the three keypress-free edits fail) and `test_the_task_inputs_are_never_run_outputs_and_clear_on_every_edit` (the wiring). Inferred from the wiring, not reproduced, not changed: an edit made while Run Diagnosis is in flight clears only the result shown before it, so the answer then shows the older query's result under the edited box (either box; the run and the edit's listener are two queued events of their own groups). A check of the result's query against the boxes once it lands would close it.
- **Re-checked (verification, fcc3dea)**: the in-flight edit reproduced in Chromium (the real panel and shell, a gene task model's stand-ins, the run's answer held on the server): a result shown, Run again, the HPO box or the genes box edited while held; the edit cleared the result shown, then the answer to the older query was drawn under the edited box and stayed. Fixed at 0149db5: the run keeps the boxes as it read them (`query_inputs`: the HPO box, the genes box, the cohort's pick), and a check chained after Run Diagnosis (`answered_inputs`, outside the queue) clears a result whose boxes changed through `phenotypes_changed`, the one clearing path; a matching result is left with its model identity; no second touched record. An input method's composition committed in either box clears the result as typing does (already so on fcc3dea). Tests: `test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_an_input_methods_text_and_an_edit_during_a_run_clear_the_result_shown` (browser) and `::test_a_result_stays_only_while_the_inputs_are_the_ones_it_answered`, both failing on fcc3dea. Left: the older answer is drawn for one request's time before the check clears it.
- **Re-checked (verification fix stage, B73's residual)**: the older answer is drawn for one request's time before `answered_inputs` clears it. It cannot be kept from being drawn by the same root without a second mechanism: Gradio writes an event's outputs as its answer arrives, and a `.then` step, where the boxes are read as they are now, starts only after. Moving the draw into that step (the run's answer kept in a State, drawn or cleared by the check) only moves the window: an edit made while the check is in flight then finds no result to clear (`phenotypes_changed` sees the State before the draw), and the check draws the older answer for good, which is worse than a flash. Keeping it from ever being drawn needs a check in the browser before the draw (a second mechanism), so it is left as is; the result shown at rest is always the inputs' own.

#### R-5 · No echo of which HPO ids were recognised or used

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity medium · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: Nothing showed which HPO ids were recognised from the input or used by the run.
- **At ba6e85e**: I typed 'HP:0001250 Seizure, HP:0001263, Hypotonia, HP:9999999, HP:0001250'. Before Run, nothing under the box says which ids were recognised. After Run, the only echo is the server's warnings line: 'Unknown phenotype: HP:9999999; Removed 1 repeated phenotype entry; each phenotype is scored once.' That line already existed at f9202b6. The name 'Hypotonia' was dropped without a word. There is no 'As used' list with names. The report's 'Query phenotypes' lists the ids as parsed, the duplicate and the unknown id included, with no used / not-used split. The CSV has no such column.
- **Done when**: Under the HPO box a live line says how many ids were recognised and which tokens are not ids; after a run an 'As used' row lists terms with names and a 'Not used (n)' list gives a reason per id; the CSV and report carry the same split.
- **Done (T02)**: the live line under the box, from its one change listener: "4 HPO ids read. ▲ Not an id, so not sent: “Hypotonia”" (`hpo_echo_html`; an entry is split at line breaks, commas and semicolons, and an id's name in its own entry is the id's). After a run, "Used (n)" and "Not used (n)" with a reason per entry (`phenotype_account`, from the server's `phenotype_input`: an entry sent that is neither used nor unknown is one the service removed as a repeat; when that does not add up to its count, the count is said instead), and the report says the same.
- **Fix (T06-D)**: after a run the "Used (n)" row (the row as used) names each term, name first and its id muted (`term_html`), from the served graph while it is the result's own (`served_status.node_names`, by `kg_sha256`; ids alone otherwise); "Not used (n)" keeps a reason per entry. The CSV carries the same split on every row, `phenotypes_used` and `phenotypes_not_used` (`not_reported` for a response with no account), after the candidate columns; the report names the terms used ("Seizure (HP:0001250), …"). Governed result semantics unchanged: the server's account is read as given.
- **Sources**: UX:207 (appendix 5); RJ audits[0].findings[12]; UX:123 (section 4 Diagnosis: 'the input echo')
- **Closed at**: e1eeb69 (names, CSV), with b22829f and 26b3501 (the live line, the split, the report). Tests: `test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_a_run_names_its_terms_from_its_own_graph_only`, `::test_the_csv_and_report_carry_the_used_and_not_used_split`, `test_results_export.py::test_the_diagnosis_csv_is_byte_identical_to_before_the_cell_rules_moved` (its pin written again with the two columns; every other cell checked unchanged) and the T02 tests; the new ones fail on d92b329. Seen on a CPU demo service from the worktree (scratch HOME/XDG, M1, the demo's disease model served) at d92b329 (port 9160) and after (9161), the audit's query 'HP:0001250 Seizure, HP:0001263, Hypotonia, HP:9999999, HP:0001250': "Used (2) Seizure HP:0001250, Global developmental delay HP:0001263" (`after_evidence_1850x960.png`, `t06d/diagnosis-results/shots/` in the session scratchpad); the service's exported CSV had both columns and its report "Phenotypes used: Seizure (HP:0001250), …". Not in the done-when, left as it was: the server's warnings line still names the unknown id and the repeat count beside "Not used".

#### B29 · Dataset building and management not available in the WebUI

- **State**: outside this round's scope (owner to decide) · found by the owner · page: datasets
- **Problem**: The owner placed dataset management and building under Prepare (2026-10-19). Building is still command-line only: the Datasets page is read-only and its BUILD_LINE points to scripts/build_knowledge_graph.py --generate-samples and scripts/setup_demo.py (datasets_page.py:79-83). It needs the job runner, the GPU lock and D8, so it is a new product surface.
- **At ba6e85e**: Recorded 2026-10-19, not started, scheduled 'Later'.
- **Why this state**: Dataset building in the WebUI is listed as a deferred new product surface in tasks_def.md (needs job runner, GPU lock and D8). Checked in the code at T06-D1 (2026-11-16): unchanged; the page lists and reads only, and its one build line names `scripts/build_knowledge_graph.py --generate-samples` and `scripts/setup_demo.py` (`datasets_page.BUILD_LINE`).
- **Owner's choice** (what Prepare › Datasets offers beyond reading the server's list; (b) and (c) may be chosen together, and each is a new product surface, not a defect of this page): (a) **command line only, as now**: no code change; a dataset is built, renamed or removed on the server by someone with shell access, and the page stays a read-only list with its one build line. (b) **build from the page**: an operator-only action (D8) under Prepare › Datasets that starts a build through the one job runner and writes a new workspace (graph, tensors, samples, manifest, dataset record, build record) under the workspaces root; it needs a build job kind with its progress, Cancel and log, a rule for the GPU lock (whether a build may run while training or a served model holds the GPU), the source files present on the server (the annotation files and ontologies, or the demo's generator), a refusal for a name already taken, and the guides and the deployment probe extended; the read-only list stays as it is. (c) **manage listed workspaces from the page** (rename, archive or delete; no building): operator-only (D8), writing in the workspaces root; a workspace holds its runs' checkpoints under `runs/` and the registry's data versions are mapped against its graph, so an act that removes or renames one must say what it takes with it, or be refused while anything names it. No recommendation is recorded.
- **Sources**: docs/working/BACKLOG.md:987 (item 29); docs/working/BACKLOG.md:183 (§0 'Later, on their own schedule'); docs/working/PLAN_TEST_RESULTS.md:2387-2389; docs/working/ops-wiring-and-ui-ux/README.md §3 point 8 ('Not in this round: ... a data builder'); src/webui/components/datasets_page.py:79-83
- **Closed at**: —

**UI-16, diagnosis**

#### B62 · Patients-like-me rows show reference patient details against D7's aggregates-only rule

- **State**: outside this round's scope (owner to decide) · found by an internal review · severity decision · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: Patients-like-me rows show reference patients' ids, terms, diagnoses; D7 says aggregates only for an institutional cohort.
- **At ba6e85e**: From the code: a patients-like-me result's rows show Research ID, Similarity, Diagnosis and Causal genes, and its details list every recorded HPO term. D7 is still undecided. The demo cannot show it: there is no patients-like-me model and the registry is empty.
- **Done when**: The owner's D7 decision is recorded, and the patients-like-me result follows it (e.g. only aggregates for an institutional cohort), verified by a renderer unit test on a sample result.
- **Why this state**: a policy decision of the owner's (PLAN_TEST_RESULTS D7, BACKLOG 62), not implemented here by the T06 brief. Checked in the code at T06 (2026-11-16): unchanged. A patients-like-me result's table shows Rank, Research ID, Similarity, Diagnosis and Causal genes (`diagnosis_panel.py`), its details every recorded HPO term, and the CSV and the report carry the same; `POST /api/v1/diagnose` (open to every request that can run a diagnosis) may name any registered cohort version as the reference (`routes/diagnose.py`, `_reference_cohort`), whatever its role; nothing enforces "synthetic only".
- **Owner's choice** (one, before any institutional cohort is named as a reference): (a) **patient-level rows allowed**: D7 is amended to let a named reference cohort's patients be shown, with who may see them and which fields may be exported; no code changes, but every browser that runs a query sees each reference patient's research id, terms and diagnosis or gene, and so do the CSV and report. (b) **aggregates only for an institutional cohort**: the result for such a cohort shows counts and similarity distributions per diagnosis or gene, never a research id or a patient's terms; the table, details, CSV, report and the API's answer change (`diagnosis_panel`'s patients-like-me renderer and exports, `routes/diagnose.py`), with a renderer test on a sample result; synthetic reference cohorts may keep rows. (c) **such a cohort may not be a reference**: `_reference_cohort` refuses a version whose role is institutional (or anything but an approved reference role), and the Diagnosis select offers only those; rows stay as they are for the cohorts that may be references. The reviewer's 2026-10-29 recommendation, not a decision: (c) for the institutional acceptance cohort, then a separate reference cohort approved with its purpose, roles and exportable fields, and D7 amended to match.

#### R-1d · Diagnosis table has no phenotype-match count (k/N)

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity high (finding 1 as a whole; this part was rated with it) · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: Review finding 1 recorded that the candidate table 'still has no phenotype match count'. Its fix step 2 asked for Rank | Disease | Score | Phenotypes k/N | Evidence, with k/N computed in the renderer from the query and matching_phenotypes and no API change. The Diagnosis audit's overall verdict lists 'a k/N phenotype column' among the structural changes, and research DX-01 asks the same. Section 3 #4 records it as excluded from M5 ('M5; no phenotype column; the repeat Later'), but section 4's Later list for Diagnosis does not carry it and neither does BACKLOG 52, so it fell out of every plan. At ba6e85e the disease table is still Rank, Disease, Score, Evidence (diagnosis_panel.py:1731-1733). The matching phenotypes appear only in the selected candidate's details segment.
- **At ba6e85e**: Left out of M5 on purpose (UX:65) and never put into section 4's Later list, BACKLOG 52 or any ops-round stage. Nothing since. Still absent at ba6e85e.
- **Done when**: The Diagnosis candidate table has a Phenotypes k/N column computed in the renderer from the query and matching_phenotypes, pinned by a unit test.
- **Fix (T06-D)**: the disease table is Rank, Disease, Score, Phenotypes, Evidence. Phenotypes is "k / N" (`diagnosis_panel.phenotype_match`): N the query's phenotypes as used (the server's `phenotype_input.used`; an older response, the distinct ids sent), k those among the row's `matching_phenotypes`; computed in the renderer, no API change; the words in the cell's title, an ⓘ on the header (`dx-phenotypes`), "not reported" when neither is known; the report's table the same. The column made the table's min-content 570 px: the evidence label now wraps first (495 px) and the results panel's minimum is that table (`--shep-panel-min` 532 px, was 490), so the page stacks below 1280 px wide instead of scrolling the table (before: side by side from below 1240 px).
- **Sources**: docs/working/webui-ux-review-2026-10-25/review.json audits[0].findings[0] (evidence: 'still no phenotype match count'; fix step 2); docs/working/webui-ux-review-2026-10-25/review.json audits[0].overall ('Diagnosis table: ... a k/N phenotype column'); docs/working/webui-ux-review-2026-10-25/review.json research[3].principles[0] (DX-01); docs/working/WEBUI_UX_REVIEW_2026-10-25.md:65 (section 3 #4: 'M5; no phenotype column'); docs/working/WEBUI_UX_REVIEW_2026-10-25.md:123 (section 4 Diagnosis Later list, which omits it); src/webui/components/diagnosis_panel.py:1731-1733
- **Closed at**: e1eeb69. #### R-3 · Evidence uses 'Mode A' jargon; phenotypes and paths show bare ids

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity medium · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: Candidate evidence was headed with internal Mode A/B jargon; phenotypes and paths showed bare ids before names.
- **At ba6e85e**: Evidence view: 'Strong path support', the summary in a quote, then the heading 'Mode A — Direct path evidence' and the line 'Shortest: 1 hop · 5 direct paths, listed under Paths ›', then supporting-gene chips. 'Mode A' is the name of an old-pipeline mode retired in R2 (BACKLOG 659). Matching phenotypes view: a bare 'HP:0001263' and the 34-word MATCHING_NOTE. Paths view: bare ids only, e.g. 'HP:0001250 → CDKL5 → MONDO:0012091'. The names are available: the same candidate's Explanation view says 'Global developmental delay (HP:0001263)', and kg.json holds every node's name.
- **Done when**: Candidate evidence shows key-value rows without 'Mode' wording with the narrative folded; phenotypes and path steps show term names first with ids muted; matching phenotypes are split annotated/not annotated with counts; paths show the 3 shortest as named chips with the rest behind a disclosure.
- **Fix (T06-D)**: Evidence answers with its label (status mark), then one summary list: Shortest path, Direct paths ("5 · listed under Paths ›"), Supporting genes; the server's narrative is folded under "Written summary ›"; evidence found by no path keeps its narrative as the answer's sentence (the page's own second sentence for it goes). No "Mode" wording, in the report either. Matching phenotypes splits the phenotypes used into "Annotated to this disease (k)" and "Not annotated (n)". Paths shows the three shortest (sorted by length, the server's order within one) as chips of named steps, the rest under "More paths (n) ›". Every term and step reads name first, its id muted (`term_html`): the names are the served graph's (`served_status.node_names`), read once with the result and only while the graph served is the result's own by its `kg_sha256`; otherwise the id alone. A gene, whose name is its symbol, is said once.
- **Sources**: UX:205 (appendix 3); RJ audits[0].findings[14]; UX:123-124 (section 4 Diagnosis: evidence as key-value rows, terms and paths name first)
- **Closed at**: e1eeb69. Tests: `test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_evidence_is_an_answer_then_facts_with_the_narrative_folded`, `::test_matching_phenotypes_are_split_annotated_or_not_name_first`, `::test_paths_show_the_three_shortest_named_and_the_rest_behind_more`, `::test_a_run_names_its_terms_from_its_own_graph_only` (the real served pipeline; another digest reads no name) and the browser test above (no "Mode A"); each fails on d92b329. Seen on a CPU demo service from the worktree (scratch HOME/XDG, M1, the demo's disease model served) at d92b329 (port 9160) and after (9161), the audit's query 'HP:0001250 Seizure, HP:0001263, Hypotonia, HP:9999999, HP:0001250': before "HP:0001263" and "HP:0001250 → CDKL5 → MONDO:0012091"; after "Global developmental delay HP:0001263" and "Seizure HP:0001250 → CDKL5 → CDKL5 deficiency disorder MONDO:0012091" (`before_/after_phenotypes_*`, `before_/after_paths_*` at 1850×960 and 960×1080, `t06d/diagnosis-results/shots/` in the session scratchpad).

#### OWN-SP · Gene table shows component scores by default; no opt-in score facets

- **State**: outside this round's scope (owner to decide) · found by the owner · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: At the recheck the owner asked why the table still showed an SP column.
- **At ba6e85e**: The disease table reads Rank, Disease, Score, Evidence: there is no SP column. That is because a disease task model serves no SP (K8-4, f4a8c0f). No facet list exists, so no other score can be added as an opt-in column or filter. Inferred from code (the demo has no gene model): a gene task model's table shows 'Model similarity' and 'SP similarity' columns by default beside Score.
- **Done when**: The gene task table shows Rank, Gene, Score, Evidence by default, with Model similarity and SP similarity available only as opt-in columns/filters from a facet list.
- **Why this state**: score facets are work item B-1b (`docs/DISEASE_SCORER_POLICY.md` §1.1–§1.3, conditions C1–C10), the owner's policy, decided in direction but not scheduled, with its selection limit, storage and export bounds and latency target still [OPEN] (§1.3); the T06 brief leaves it to the owner. Checked in the code at T06 (2026-11-16): unchanged. The disease table has no SP column (K8-4); a gene result's table shows Model similarity and SP similarity beside Score by default (`diagnosis_panel.py`), and its details and CSV carry both.
- **Owner's choice**: (a) **schedule B-1b for the gene table**: default columns Rank, Gene, Score, Evidence; Model similarity and SP similarity offered only from a facet list as opt-in columns or filters under C1–C10 (the canonical rank kept, "showing n of m" while filtered, an unavailable SP a state of its own, filtering and counts over the whole selected set); needs §1.3's open values decided first, and changes the gene table, its facet controls and their tests (the CSV keeps every column). (b) **keep today's columns** until B-1b is scheduled: nothing changes; the row stays open. (c) **an interim without facets**: the two component columns leave the table and stay in the candidate's details and the CSV; a renderer change only, no filter, so it does not meet C1–C10 and B-1b stays open.

#### B48 · Training and inference exclusion on the GPU not verified

- **State**: awaiting hardware · found by the owner · severity requirement · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: Owner's provisional requirement: while training runs, Diagnosis and inference are locked; while a pipeline is loaded, Training is locked.
- **At ba6e85e**: Seen here: the card has '■ Stop inference' with a two-step question, and the header chip reads 'GPU jobs: none'. CPU serving takes no lock: /api/v1/pipeline/status returned serving:'serving' and holds_gpu_lock:false. That matches the owner's 2026-11-12 choice to lock the GPU, not the pages. Not seen, from the code: on CUDA, serving holds the one GPU lock and GPU jobs are refused with a message naming inference (training_manager.py:1072 serving_refusal; chips.py:126).
- **Why this state**: Serving holds the GPU lock only on CUDA; checking that training and inference exclude each other needs a CUDA device (GB10 acceptance W12 not run).
- **Done when**: On a CUDA device, while serving, a GPU job is refused with a message naming inference and the header chip shows it, and while training, loading for inference is refused likewise.
- **Verified (T06, 2026-11-16, in the code; no CUDA here)**: the record holds. A CUDA load takes the GPU lock before it reads a candidate and then reads the runner's slot (`AppState`'s load in `src/api/main.py`); while a training holds the lock it is refused "CUDA inference is refused: the GPU is in use by a training job (train_model.py, pid …, as it described itself). Load again once it has ended.", and in the window before the job's process takes the lock, by the runner's `serving_refusal` ("…the GPU is in use, since training is running in this service's job runner (job …)"). While serving on CUDA a runner start is refused naming this service's inference (`_slot_refusal_locked`, `gpu_lock.held_by_inference`) and the chip reads "GPU: inference (this service)" with where to stop it (`chips.gpu_chip_from`). The probe's W12 checks the serving half on CUDA; neither W12 nor the GB10 UI steps check the second half (a load refused while training runs).
- **GB10 check step** (one run, on the candidate slot): (1) the probe, `scripts/probe_deployment.py --m2-acceptance --device cuda --work-dir <a new directory> --real-workspace <a new copy> --real-reference-checkpoint <its model-02 file> --workspaces-root <the copy's root>`: W12's facts must read `loaded.holds_gpu_lock` true, `while_serving.runner_start` "refused (409), naming this service's inference" and `while_serving.training_cli` "refused (exit 1), naming the inference holder; nothing written" (`ops-wiring-and-ui-ux/GB10_ACCEPTANCE.md` §2). (2) GB10_ACCEPTANCE §4 UI step 3: load a disease child on Diagnosis; the header chip reads "GPU: inference (this service)"; a Training start is refused naming inference and "stop it with Stop inference on Diagnosis"; Stop inference; the chip reads "GPU jobs: none". (3) The half no probe checks: start a short Training run on CUDA from the Training page; while the chip reads "GPU job: training (job runner)", Diagnosis › Change model › Load of a disease child is refused with one of the two refusals above and nothing is loaded; once the run has ended, the same Load succeeds. Record what each step showed in the round's GB10 evidence directory.
- **Sources**: BL:1006 (item 48); OPS:62-64 (section 3 point 1)
- **Closed at**: —

#### T08-flow-3 · Tab reaches the Explanations checkbox before its ⓘ, the reverse of what is drawn

- **State**: code-verified · found by the T08 browser flow · severity low · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: Diagnosis › Options draws "Explanations ⓘ ☑", but Tab reached the checkbox before its ⓘ (WCAG 2.4.3).
- **At 71587aa**: Diagnosis › Options at 1850×960 and 960×1080: stops 7-10 are Help: Top-K, the Top-K field, the Explanations checkbox (x 162), then Help: Explanations (x 130). Evidence: scratchpad/t08/C/json/c6d-keyboard.json, scratchpad/t08/C/shots/kbd/diagnosis-*-09.png, -10.png.
- **Done when**: A browser test Tabs through each checkbox row with an ⓘ, on every page, and meets its ⓘ and its box in the order they are drawn; it fails before.
- **Sources**: the T08 browser flow (scratchpad/t08/C/json/c6d-keyboard.json); R-24 (the Options grid: keys, ⓘ and controls in three columns)
- **Closed at**: 0ca486c. Root: Gradio puts a checkbox's box before its words and the shared help adds the ⓘ after the words; the Options grid then draws words, ⓘ, box by explicit grid columns, so the page's order and the drawn order differ. Every other checkbox with an ⓘ (Training's Mixed precision and its Model accordion's five, Fine-tune's Mixed precision, Test's two, Settings' torch.compile) is drawn in Gradio's own order, box, words, ⓘ, which Tab already follows; Training's Continue drawer's Mixed precision is the same setting field. Fix in the shared help: a container whose fields are drawn as rows of key, ⓘ and control says so (`help.KEY_FIRST_CLASS`), and the help keeps each such field's control after its ⓘ in the page. Diagnosis' Options row carries it; its grid is drawn as before (R-24's one left edge and two columns unchanged). Test: `test_webui_help.py::test_tab_reaches_each_checkboxs_i_and_box_in_the_order_they_are_drawn` (the served dashboard in operator mode, 1850×960 and 960×1080: 11 checkboxes on five pages). It fails on e0e4237 (Explanations: box, then ⓘ). Screens (scratchpad/t08fix/U1/shots/): before/after_flow3-tab-after-topk_1850x960.png and _960x1080.png (the stop after Top-K: the box before, the ⓘ with its preview after).

#### T08-flow-5 · The Load drawer's Model Facts say "Trained on the selected workspace's current dataset" twice

- **State**: code-verified · found by the T08 browser flow · severity low · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: A listed file trained on the selected workspace's current dataset said so twice in its Model Facts: Identity's last row and the Data section's only row (rule 1.2).
- **At 71587aa**: Diagnosis › Load a model…: 5 of 5 files chosen showed "Trained on the selected workspace's current dataset" in Identity and again in Data. Evidence: scratchpad/t08/C/json/c7-loaddrawer.json, scratchpad/t08/C/shots/c7-b-ft-fu-*.png.
- **Done when**: A unit test counts the fact once in Model Facts; it fails before.
- **Sources**: the T08 browser flow (scratchpad/t08/C/json/c7-loaddrawer.json); SK:23-25 (rule 1.2, each fact once)
- **Closed at**: d03c00d. Root: `renderers.model_facts_html` drew the relation in both lists when `ModelFacts.current_dataset` is set (Identity's dataset row in place of the fingerprint, and Data's "Trained on"). Fix: Identity then draws no dataset row; Data says it once. A file trained on another dataset is unchanged (its fingerprint whole with its Copy in Identity, its workspaces in Data). Every caller shares it: the Load drawer's and Models' facts (`model_list.facts_html`) and the Diagnosis card's served facts (never `current_dataset`). Test: `test_webui_renderers.py::test_model_facts_say_a_file_trained_on_the_current_dataset_once` (with and without Uses; fails on e0e4237, counted 2). Seen in the real app on a copy of the T08 workspace: the b-ft-full-disease last.pt facts say it 2 times before, 1 after (scratchpad/t08fix/U3/json/before-look.json, after-look.json, `facts.said`). Screens: scratchpad/t08fix/U3/shots/before-drawer-facts-w.png and after-drawer-facts-w.png (1850×960), the -n pair at 960×1080.


#### T08-flow-12 · A Load refused for its device leaves the Diagnosis card with no reason

- **State**: code-verified · found by the T08 browser flow, recorded by the verification of fcc3dea · severity medium · page: diagnosis
- **Problem**: On a host without CUDA and no SHEPHERD_DEVICE, Diagnosis › Load a model… › Load this model was refused in the drawer in the device's words, but the card, its Details and the status route said only "Load refused at <time> · The operator's reload response and the service log say why". The GPU lock's refusal, decided at the same point, reached them in its own words.
- **At fcc3dea**: `run_reload` recorded `load.lock_refusal or RELOAD_REFUSED` (src/api/routes/pipeline.py ~583); the device, refused by `resolve_serving_device` in `_reload` (~603) before any path is read, never reached the record, and start-up recorded it as `BUILD_REFUSED`. Its words also named the variable in the main text ("Set SHEPHERD_DEVICE=cpu …"), and a lock refusal's own "Ref:" was followed by the load hint. Evidence: scratchpad/t08/B/logs/b8-load-nodevice.out, scratchpad/t08/B/shots/b8-nodevice-03-after-load-w.png, b8-nodevice-04-card-details-w.png.
- **Done when**: Every refusal decided before a path is read (the device, the GPU lock) reaches the card, its Details and the status route in its own words through one channel, the words plain first and the variable only in a trailing Ref, said last on each surface; a refusal that may name a path keeps pointing at the log; a test fails on fcc3dea; a look at the card before and after.
- **Sources**: the T08 browser flow (scratchpad/t08/B/logs/b8-load-nodevice.out); the integrator's brief for the verification's fix stage, item 1; SK:§5 (codes only in a trailing "Ref:")
- **Closed at**: d03ed03. Root: the open record had one path-free channel, the lock's (`ServingLoad.lock_refusal`), and the device was decided outside it. Fix: `ServingLoad.settle` decides both, the device by the serving rule and then the lock, and keeps either refusal in `ServingLoad.refusal`; the reload route and start-up both call it, and record `refusal or RELOAD_REFUSED` (`BUILD_REFUSED` at start-up). The device's words now say what is wrong and what to do first, the variable in the Ref ("… On a development host, start the service with the CPU device (Settings › Stop service). Ref: SHEPHERD_DEVICE=cpu, or device 'cpu' in a reload request (deployment guide §4.8)"); `main.split_ref` keeps any reason's Ref last, after the load hint in the not-serving reason and after "still being served" in the reload's answer. Guides (en, zh) §4.8 quote the new words. Tests: `test_serving_device.py::test_a_load_refused_for_its_device_says_why_on_every_open_surface` (no device and `cuda`; fails on fcc3dea), `::test_a_refusals_ref_is_said_last_on_every_open_surface` (the lock's Ref, a load and start-up; fails on fcc3dea), `::test_a_refusal_that_may_name_a_path_still_points_at_the_log` (passes before and after), and `::test_a_startup_refused_for_its_device_answers_503` now finds the device's words, no path, on the open route. Screens of the card in the served dashboard (device refused, nothing served): before scratchpad/verify/fix/r2/flow12/shots/before_card_1850x960.png, before_card-details_1850x960.png and the 390×844 pair; after: after_card_*.png, after_card-details_*.png.

**UI-16, finetune**

#### NEW-training-finetune-6 · Emptied task setting reads 'must be set, not null'

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-11-16 audit · page: finetune
- **Duplicate of**: OPS-R2 (closed with it)
- **At ba6e85e**: Fine-tune: an emptied task setting reads 'Not valid: Task loss weight must be set, not null.' ('null' is a programming word on the page; finetune-form-checks.json)
- **Done when**: Emptying a Fine-tune task setting shows a refusal without the word 'null' (e.g. 'Task loss weight must be set.'), and the reason also shows beside Start.
- **Sources**: found by the 2026-11-16 audit at ba6e85e
- **Closed at**: f9512cf, a37d09f. The one value check (`training_fields.value_problem`) refuses an empty value as '<field> must be set.'; Fine-tune also draws what Start would refuse beside Start (`finetune_page.refused_html`, `#ft_refused`: '▲ Start would refuse: Learning rate must be set.'), refreshed by every field's input, the task choice and Reset. Tests: `test_webui_finetune_page.py::test_an_emptied_task_number_is_refused_before_start_and_never_said_to_be_the_reference` (no 'null'; the line beside Start) and `test_training_fields.py` (fails on b2732ea). Seen at 1850x960 and 960x1080 with Learning rate emptied: before 'Not valid: Learning rate must be set, not null.' and nothing beside Start; after 'Not valid: Learning rate must be set.' and the refusal beside Start. Screens: scratchpad/t06b/continue-finetune/shots/before/finetune_emptied_1850x960.png, scratchpad/t06b/continue-finetune/shots/after/finetune_emptied_1850x960.png.
- **Re-checked (T06 review fix, c2588b4)**: after a click on Start the refusal was said twice beside Start: the line '▲ Start would refuse: Learning rate must be set.' (`refused_html`) and the notice '✕ Not started: Learning rate must be set.' (`_start`'s sentence). Start's refusal of the form's own values (`sent_settings`' problems) now takes that line, as the ✕ of a refusal ('✕ Not started: Learning rate must be set.'), and adds no notice (`_start` says whether its refusal is the form's values; any other refusal stays the notice, the line left as it is); the next input says it again as what Start would refuse. The 'Set by you' line keeps its 'Not valid: …' under the fields: this row's Done when asks for the reason beside Start too. Tests: `test_webui_finetune_page.py::test_an_emptied_task_number_is_refused_before_start_and_never_said_to_be_the_reference`, `::test_start_refuses_what_it_cannot_send_and_starts_nothing` (each case: the form's values beside Start, the rest in the notice) and `::test_the_page_follows_the_contract` (Start writes `#ft_refused`), failing on 54d5f4b. Seen on the CPU demo at 1850x960 and 960x1080, Disease ticked, Learning rate emptied, Start clicked: before Start's row said 'must be set' twice ('▲ Start would refuse: …' and '✕ Not started: …'); after once ('✕ Not started: Learning rate must be set.'). Screens: scratchpad/t06b/fixer/shots/before/finetune_refused_1850x960.png, scratchpad/t06b/fixer/shots/after/finetune_refused_1850x960.png (and _960x1080).

#### T08-flow-6 · The Fine-tune job card's table is wider than its panel at 1850×960

- **State**: code-verified · found by the T08 browser flow · severity medium · page: finetune
- **Problem**: The job card's table kept its heads and its run name on one line, so it was wider than its card; its last column was cut at the panel's edge, with no scroll bar.
- **At 71587aa**: Fine-tune at 1850×960, running and done: table 659 px in a 515 px card, its right edge at 1961 px against the panel's 1834 px, `overflow-x: visible` on every ancestor up to `#ft_card`; "Validation (Trainer)" and its value cut. At 960×1080 it fit. Evidence: scratchpad/t08/B/shots/b3-04-full-done-*.png, scratchpad/t08/B/shots/b3-06-probe-card-done-w.png, scratchpad/t08/B/json/flow.json (s3b_card_probe.card_geometry_1850).
- **Done when**: A browser test at 1850×960 (running and done), 1536×760 and 960×1080 measures the table inside its card and every cell's text unclipped; it fails before. Before and after screens.
- **Sources**: the T08 browser flow (scratchpad/t08/B/json/flow.json, scratchpad/t08/B/shots/b3-06-probe-card-done-w.png); SK:38-40 (§2, one scroll region); SK:85 (§5, numbers whole)
- **Closed at**: a6aae90. Root: the card's table kept every head on one line (`th` `white-space: nowrap`) and its run name whole on one line (the shared `.shep-ident`: no wrap, an ellipsis past a 40ch cap), so its narrowest layout was wider than the job panel. Fix, in the card's table (`.shep-ft-table`, drawn on Fine-tune only; Training's and Test's job cards draw no such table): a head wraps at its spaces and the run name after its own hyphens, never inside a token (as Compare's run ids, B72-C4); numbers never break; nothing is cut and nothing scrolls sideways. Test: `test_webui_finetune_page.py::test_the_job_cards_table_fits_its_card_with_every_cell_whole` (the page served whole, its card drawn by its own poll, running and done, at the three sizes; fails on e0e4237, table right edge 1913 px against the card's 1660); the pin of the old 40ch cap in `::test_the_cards_run_is_its_name_part_with_the_whole_id_in_its_title` now checks the same fact (the name shown whole). Seen in the real app, a 1-epoch CPU fine-tune of b-base on a copy of the T08 workspace, running and done: the table 515 px in its 515 px card at 1850×960, 420 in 420 at 1536×760, 894 in 894 at 960×1080, no cell cut, no sideways scroll (scratchpad/t08fix/U3/json/after-ftcard-real.json). Screens: scratchpad/t08fix/U3/shots/before-ftcard-running-1850x960.png, before-ftcard-done-1850x960.png and the after- pair (the served page; the -1536x760 and -960x1080 shots beside them), after-ftcard-real-running-w.png and after-ftcard-real-done-w.png (the real app; -n at 960×1080).
- **Re-checked (review of a6aae90, 6f30c2b)**: a run name with no hyphen to wrap at (one word), or the whole id of a directory not named by its start time, made the table wider than its card again: a6aae90 dropped the shared identifier's cap, and the Run column's narrowest width was its longest token. In the served page at 1850×960 (its card 420 px), a 40-character one-word name (its child ended, with its best saves) and a 47-character legacy id: the table 752 px in the 420 px card, the panel scrolling sideways (4b5cee6); 752 in 702 at 960×1080. Fix (`finetune_page.py`, the card's table only): the run is drawn in a one-track grid, `minmax(RUN_FLOOR, max-content)`, so the column's narrowest is a floor (60 px, seven characters of the code font), not its longest token; past it the column takes its share of the card up to the whole name, the name still wraps after its hyphens, and a token wider than its share ends in the shared identifier's ellipsis, its whole id in its title. Test: the same fit test's third state (both tokens) at the three sizes: the table inside its card, only those two cut, each titled with its whole id; running and done cut nothing; fails on 4b5cee6 and e0e4237. Limits: at a 420 px card a one-word name of a child that ended shows about six characters (the done table's other columns take about 340 px); at 1440×1600 the 47-character id is cut by a few characters where a6aae90 showed it whole. Not changed here (as at 4b5cee6): in the 406 px card of a 440 px job panel (1280×900, 1366×620) a done table is 416 px (415 before), 10 px into the panel's padding, nothing cut, no scroll; at 390×844 the table is wider than its card in every state. Screens (the served page): scratchpad/t08fix/fix-ui/shots/before-tokens-1850x960.png and after-tokens-1850x960.png (-1536x760 and -960x1080 beside them; the done state as before-done-*/after-done-*); the measures in scratchpad/t08fix/fix-ui/logs/before-measure.json (the three sizes) and after-measure.json (nine window sizes).

#### R-10+R-43+B52K · Heading ⓘ bubbles can cover the region they explain

- **State**: code-verified · found by the 2026-10-25 review · severity medium · page: help
- **Problem**: The bubble opened over the element it explains (HPO terms, Epochs) and was 360 px wide against a ~320 px budget.

A field's ⓘ takes the field's rectangle from its elem_id (help.py:263-266; d675d17) and places the bubble beside, then above, then below the field (help.py:311-317; f2bf458). Measured at 1850 x 960: the HPO terms bubble sits at x 379-699 beside its field (x 33-369), the Epochs bubble at x 300-620 beside its field (x 33-290), and Device beside its field, all with 0 px² overlap. At 960 x 1080 HPO terms and Epochs also have 0 overlap. Width is fixed: every bubble measured 320 px (BUBBLE_MAX_WIDTH, help.py:80). A topic ⓘ in a region's heading is not fixed. Its protected area is only the line it sits in (help.py:277, `closest('… label, div')`). For 'Model in use' that is the 138 x 22 px title (33,442 to 171,464). The pinned bubble (181,401 to 501,952) covers 55% of the card it explains ('Not loaded', 'Nothing has been loaded', Details ›) and 39% of Run Diagnosis and its 'Disabled until…' reason. At 960 x 1080 it covers 35% of the card and 40% of Run Diagnosis. This is the same case the review cited (02-diagnosis-help-bubble.png). The R10 ⓘ sits in a result row and needs a report, so it cannot be seen in the demo. By the code its key-value row is protected (help.py:268-276).
- **Done when**: A ⓘ in a region heading (the Diagnosis card first) places its bubble outside the region it explains (closest region container), with 0 overlap measured in a browser test.
- **Sources**: UX:212 (appendix 10); RJ audits[0].findings[4]; UX:245 (appendix 43); RJ audits[1].findings[10]; UX:51-52; UX:121-122; BL:1010 (item 52 (K), K6 closing smoke); SK:118
- **Recorded (T04 measure, 2cd2d20)**: a field's bubble read a 0x0 box for a field drawn with display: contents (Diagnosis' Top-K and Explanations since b405973) and covered the ⓘ itself; fixed in help.py's field rectangle with R-24. The heading case this row names was closed separately at f44f754 (the region rule above); the shared check flags a bubble only over its own focused ⓘ, not over the region, so the region case stays covered by the help browser test. Both fixes are kept together in the integration merge.
- **Closed at**: f44f754. A ⓘ that is no field's, table row's or key-value row's and sits inside a region marked `help.REGION_CLASS` keeps the whole region uncovered when one of the four sides fits, else the line it sits in, as before; the Diagnosis card is marked. Tests: `test_webui_help.py::test_the_pinned_bubble_never_covers_its_field_and_shows_text_as_text` (browser: a card heading's ⓘ, its card 0 px² covered at 1850×960 and 420×800; the base bubble covers 24,649 px² at both) and `test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_the_cards_title_help_explains_the_card_and_its_reread_is_a_full_target`. Seen on the CPU demo (M1, no model loaded): the pinned 'Model in use' bubble beside the card at 1850×960 (395,529 to 715,933; card 17,535 to 385,685) and above it at 960×1080 (135,111 to 455,515): 0 px² over the card, Run Diagnosis and its 'Disabled until…' reason at both (30,678, 3,747 and 5,620 px² before at 1850×960). Not marked: the other heading ⓘs (Candidate details, Models › Results, Chart gestures), whose regions fill most of a column, so a bubble kept outside them would cover the results beside them; they keep their line.

#### R-29+R-83+B72 · 'Esc to close' footer on hover previews; card ↻ below 24x24

- **Problem**: Pinned bubble kept tooltip semantics, hover opened instantly, every bubble had an 'Esc to close' footer, ⓘ targets were 20 px (also R-51, R-83, BACKLOG 72).

A hover opens after 300 ms (HOVER_OPEN_MS, help.py:81, :368-373): hidden at 120 ms, shown at 500 ms. Keyboard focus opens at once. - **Closed at**: f44f754. 'Esc to close' is a pinned bubble's only (`setRole` hides the footer on a hover or focus preview). The card's ↻ is a 24×24 target (`HIT_TARGET`; a −4 px margin keeps the title row's height). Tests: `test_webui_help.py::test_the_pinned_bubble_never_covers_its_field_and_shows_text_as_text` (every ⓘ's preview has no footer and its pinned bubble has it; the base bubble shows it on hover) and `test_diagnosis_panel.py::test_the_cards_title_help_explains_the_card_and_its_reread_is_a_full_target`. Seen on the CPU demo at 1850×960 and 960×1080: the 'Model in use' preview without the footer, pinned with it; the ↻ 24×24 (23.3×16 before). The test's waits on what the page shows found a race the old fixed 350 ms sleep hid: the hide a previous ⓘ's mouseout scheduled cancelled the next ⓘ's pending preview; a pending preview now replaces the bubble instead.
- **Recorded (T08 suite fix, 5dd770e)**: a regression of the preview's lifetime, found by the shared walk: `ui_walk` errored at setup because the Diagnosis load drawer's × never closed at 390×844. The close sent no join; the pointer went down on `#shep-help-bubble > .shep-bubble-bd > p` ("Validation (Trainer)"). The walk's Tab focus walk opened that ⓘ's focus preview. The next control's scroll moved the bubble, with its ⓘ, under the resting pointer, and the browser's `mouseover`/`mouseenter` (no `mousemove`) cleared the hide the ⓘ's focusout had asked for. The preview stayed open after its focus left and covered the ×. T06-C changed no help code; its drawer changes moved where the bubble lands. Fixed in `help.py`: only the reader's pointer moving in the bubble (`mousemove`) keeps it. Test: `test_webui_help.py::test_a_focus_preview_closes_though_it_slid_under_a_resting_pointer` (browser, paused page clock, 1850×960 and 420×800): on c46a5a7 the bubble is still drawn under the pointer after 1000 ms (3 of 3 runs), after the fix it is closed. Seen on a CPU demo on port 9141, running the walk's Diagnosis stops then its drawer stops: before the fix, 2 of 3 runs failed at 390×844; after it, 3 of 3 closed (the bubble hid 250–263 ms after the focus left, the click on the ×, close in 159–211 ms). The four `ui_walk` tests pass in one run (315 s), and so do the three `acceptance_walk` tests (396 s). Not changed here: a bubble kept inside the window while its ⓘ is scrolled out of view (the stale preview was drawn at top 257 px with its ⓘ at 2653 px) is its own open row, T08-help-1.

#### T08-help-1 · A bubble whose ⓘ is scrolled out of view stays in the window over other controls

- **State**: code-verified · found by an internal review · severity low · page: help
- **Problem**: An open bubble, pinned or a preview, follows its ⓘ on a scroll but is always kept inside the window, and nothing asks whether its ⓘ is still in view, so once its ⓘ is scrolled out of view the bubble stays drawn over controls it has nothing to do with. A click on such a control lands on the bubble and does nothing; the reader has to press Esc or click elsewhere first. Found by the T08 suite fix (R-29+R-83+B72's record, 5dd770e) and kept only as a note in that closed row until the review of 03aaf86.
- **At 03aaf86**: In `help.py`'s `place()`, run on every scroll (`window.addEventListener('scroll', place, true)`, help.py:623), `x()` and `y()` clamp the bubble into the window (help.py:481-482); `at('right')` and `at('left')` take their top from `y(cy - 24, h)` (help.py:485-486), the last fallbacks are clamped by `bubble.style.top = Math.max(EDGE, chosen.top)` (help.py:525), and `fits()` checks the window and the field, never whether the ⓘ is in view. The capture-phase click handler closes an open bubble only on a click outside it (help.py:606-614). Reproduced in Chromium on a static page with the real `HELP_HEAD` and `HELP_CSS`, a field's bubble pinned by a click on its ⓘ: (a) the window scrolled by 2000 px: the ⓘ at top −1945 px, the bubble drawn at 384,8 to 660,187 over three controls at 1850×960 (99,8 to 375,187 over six at 420×800); `elementFromPoint` at the first one's centre is the bubble's `p`, a click there reaches no control and the bubble stays open. (b) the field in a region that scrolls inside (`overflow: auto`), the region scrolled by 300 px: the ⓘ (top 72 px) is in the window but out of its region's view (region top 340 px); the bubble drawn over the controls above the region, at 393,60 to 669,239 at 1850×960 and 108,122 to 384,301 at 420×800; a click on one reaches none. Screens: scratchpad/t08suite/ledger-help/shots/scrolled_1850x960.png, scrolled_420x800.png, region_scrolled_1850x960.png, region_scrolled_420x800.png (pinned_*.png: before the scroll).
- **Done when**: A bubble, pinned or a preview, whose ⓘ leaves view (out of the window, or out of the visible part of the region it scrolls in) is hidden or follows its ⓘ out of view, and never covers another control; while its ⓘ is in view its placement is as before (never over the field it explains: beside, then above, then below). A browser test at 1850×960 and a narrow size pins both cases, the window scrolled and an inner region scrolled, and a click on a control the bubble covered before the scroll reaches that control; it fails on 03aaf86.
- **Sources**: R-29+R-83+B72 (its T08 record, 5dd770e); the review of 03aaf86 (T08); SK:113 (§7: a toggletip that Esc and an outside click close); SK:39 (nothing sticky or fixed may cover a focused control; the bubble is `position: fixed`, help.py:751)
- **Re-checked (T08 flow, 71587aa)**: seen again in the real app at 960×1080. Training's Dataset ⓘ, pinned or previewed, the page scrolled by 350 px (its ⓘ at top −160 px): the bubble stayed at 358,8 to 678,256 over "Help: Chart gestures" and "Reset view". Test's Workspace ⓘ (at −112 px): the bubble over "Help: Data version" and the version's Copy (scratchpad/t08/C/json/c8h-bubble-scroll.json, scratchpad/t08/C/shots/bubble/training-n-*.png, test-n-*.png). Fixing it showed a second path to the same symptom: a bubble closed by its × gave focus back to its ⓘ mid-close, that focus opened a preview, and `close()` then cleared its ⓘ, so the preview had nothing to follow and stayed where it was when the page scrolled (on e0e4237, after × and a 2000 px scroll: drawn at the window's top, its ⓘ at −1962 px).
- **Closed at**: e4e34b1. Root: `place()` (help.py, run on every scroll) clamped the bubble into the window and never asked whether its ⓘ was in view; `close()` gave focus back mid-close, without `preventScroll`. Fix in the one shared bubble, so every ⓘ has it (fields, topics in rendered HTML, the header chips): `place()` reads whether the ⓘ is out of view as Floating UI's `hide` reads its reference (`outOfView`: wholly outside the window or an ancestor that clips its overflow, absolutely and fixed positioned boxes minded) and then does not draw the bubble (visibility hidden, so no click lands on it). The bubble stays open (WCAG 1.4.13, persistent) and is drawn again where it belongs when its ⓘ is back. Focus inside it moves to its ⓘ with `preventScroll`; Esc closes it and, while it is not drawn, still reaches the page. `close()` resets its state first, then gives focus back with `preventScroll`; that focus opens nothing. Test: `test_webui_help.py::test_a_bubble_whose_i_left_view_is_not_drawn_over_other_controls` (browser, 1850×960 and 960×1080, the window scrolled by 2000 px and an inner region by 300 px, pinned and preview: not drawn, a click at its place reaches the control there, drawn again where it was with its ⓘ back; focus inside it and Esc; closed by its ×, it stays closed). It fails on e0e4237: the bubble is drawn at its clamped place and takes the click, and Esc jumps the page back to 0. Screens (scratchpad/t08fix/U1/shots/): before/after_help1-window-scrolled_960x1080.png (Training's Dataset ⓘ, the window scrolled by 338 px: drawn at 359,8 to 679,256 before, not drawn after) and before/after_help1-region-scrolled_1850x960.png (Training's Epochs ⓘ above its Parameters region: drawn at 300,432 to 620,654 before, not drawn after).
